Free GHA Titanium Hotel Status For Bank Of America’s $100,000 Rewards Tier

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

If you have $100,000 in qualifying Bank of America and Merrill balances, check your banking rewards before paying for hotel elite status. There’s a reported benefit tucked inside the program that gets you GHA Discovery Titanium, with no hotel stay required. On the November 6 Frequent Miler on the Air episode, Greg Davis-Kean said he used this himself. His status upgrade was instant and runs through December 31, 2027. I’ve called GHA my second favorite hotel loyalty program, after Hyatt. I’ve received real benefits at hotels I actually wanted to stay in. Where To Find The Bank Of America Offer Greg reports that it works for Preferred Honors and Premier members, as a one-time match. That’s based on banking relationship tier rather than specific card product. Bank of America publishes the requirements for access to its lifestyle benefits: Preferred Honors requires an eligible checking account and a combined three-month average daily balance of at least $100,000 in qualifying Bank of America deposits and/or Merrill investment accounts. Premier generally starts at $1 million. Bank of America Private Bank clients can qualify for Premier regardless of balances. Enrolled members can find lifestyle benefits under the Rewards tab in online banking or MyMerrill, or through the lifestyle benefits tile in BofA Rewards. Look in the travel offers for GHA Discovery and follow the specific activation link. The $100,000 doesn’t have to be cash in a checking account, it includes investments at Merrill count. The Value Of GHA Titanium Status GHA Discovery is the loyalty program for roughly 1,000 hotels across about 60 brands, including Kempinski, Anantara, Capella, Pan Pacific, NH and Lore Group. It’s a way for smaller hotel groups to offer recognition and rewards across a much larger collection. I covered the program’s relaunch in December 2021, when it added Discovery Dollars (essentially, points-earning, it had been primarily a recognition program before that) and made status easier to earn. Titanium is the highest published tier. Normally, Titanium takes 30 eligible nights, $15,000 in eligible spending, or stays at three qualifying brands in a calendar year. The main Titanium benefits are: A two-category room upgrade, subject to availability, and that can be a suite. 11 a.m. early check-in and 4 p.m. late checkout, both subject to availability. Request early check-in at least two days ahead. A welcome amenity. 7% back in Discovery Dollars on eligible spending. Each dollar is worth one U.S. dollar towards eligible hotel charges, and regularly earned Titanium dollars expire after 24 months. There’s also breakfast for two at participating brands on direct bookings. This has expanded since I covered the benefit’s introduction in 2024, but it still isn’t universal. Lore Group and Capella participate, as do Anantara and many others. Titanium doesn’t guarantee club lounge access. And the ability to gift Titanium to someone else requires earning the status through the normal qualifying criteria so a match alone doesn’t get you that benefit. I’ve made several GHA Discovery stays through Amex Fine Hotels and Resorts as well as Chase’s The Edit, and benefits stack. At NH Collection Palazzo dei Dogi in Venice, early check-in and 4 p.m. checkout was especially useful. At Riggs in Washington, D.C., I booked through Chase’s The Edit and combined its benefits with GHA status. My suite was the former bank boardroom, with a dining table for twelve. I also combined American Express Fine Hotels + Resorts with GHA at Kempinski Cancun, receiving an oceanfront suite and club access. Greg’s Augustine Prague story was a good experience as well. He’d booked a hotel that converted to a Kempinski so he added his new GHA number and contacted the hotel. They combined the Fine Hotels + Resorts upgrade with Titanium’s double upgrade and put him in a historic Treasury suite. He also earned D$75 from the stay. Don’t Have The Bank Relationship? There’s Still A Paid Match I’ve covered GHA’s unusually broad status match offers before, including eligibility through airline and cruise status and selected credit cards rather than just another hotel program. The current paid match is scheduled to run through November 30, 2026, with status through December 31, 2027: Titanium costs $150 and comes with D$75. Platinum costs $100 and comes with D$50. The gift dollars expire after 12 months, and there isn’t a stay challenge required. Qualifying Titanium matches include Hilton Diamond, Hyatt Globalist, Marriott Platinum and above, and IHG Platinum and above, among others. For someone who will use all D$75, the Titanium offer has a $75 net cost. You can potentially match GHA Titanium onward to other programs as well. And the easiest way to qualify in some cities would be three one night stays at different brands. GHA Discovery is the loyalty program for roughly 1,000 hotels across about 60 brands, including Kempinski, Anantara, Capella, Pan Pacific, NH and Lore Group. It’s a way for smaller hotel groups to offer recognition and rewards across a much larger collection.

I covered the program’s relaunch in December 2021, when it added Discovery Dollars (essentially, points-earning, it had been primarily a recognition program before that) and made status easier to earn.

Titanium is the highest published tier. Normally, Titanium takes 30 eligible nights, $15,000 in eligible spending, or stays at three qualifying brands in a calendar year. The main Titanium benefits are:

There’s also breakfast for two at participating brands on direct bookings. This has expanded since I covered the benefit’s introduction in 2024, but it still isn’t universal. Lore Group and Capella participate, as do Anantara and many others.

Titanium doesn’t guarantee club lounge access. And the ability to gift Titanium to someone else requires earning the status through the normal qualifying criteria so a match alone doesn’t get you that benefit. I’ve made several GHA Discovery stays through Amex Fine Hotels and Resorts as well as Chase’s The Edit, and benefits stack.

At NH Collection Palazzo dei Dogi in Venice, early check-in and 4 p.m. checkout was especially useful. At Riggs in Washington, D.C., I booked through Chase’s The Edit and combined its benefits with GHA status. My suite was the former bank boardroom, with a dining table for twelve.

I also combined American Express Fine Hotels + Resorts with GHA at Kempinski Cancun, receiving an oceanfront suite and club access.

Greg’s Augustine Prague story was a good experience as well. He’d booked a hotel that converted to a Kempinski so he added his new GHA number and contacted the hotel. They combined the Fine Hotels + Resorts upgrade with Titanium’s double upgrade and put him in a historic Treasury suite. He also earned D$75 from the stay.

I’ve covered GHA’s unusually broad status match offers before, including eligibility through airline and cruise status and selected credit cards rather than just another hotel program. The current paid match is scheduled to run through November 30, 2026, with status through December 31, 2027: Titanium costs $150 and comes with D$75. Platinum costs $100 and comes with D$50. The gift dollars expire after 12 months, and there isn’t a stay challenge required. Qualifying Titanium matches include Hilton Diamond, Hyatt Globalist, Marriott Platinum and above, and IHG Platinum and above, among others. For someone who will use all D$75, the Titanium offer has a $75 net cost. You can potentially match GHA Titanium onward to other programs as well. And the easiest way to qualify in some cities would be three one night stays at different brands. Nick Jonas Admits He 'Resents' His Diabetes Diagnosis in Candid Open Letter

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Nick Jonas is reflecting on navigating diabetes more than two decades after receiving his diagnosis.

“It’s funny how often we talk,” Jonas, 34, wrote in an open letter as part of Dexcom’s A Letter to Diabetes initiative. “Staring at my phone to see where my glucose is. Reaching for my pump to bolus because I’m about to eat something with 17 carbs. Those are just a couple of the hundreds of little decisions I make every week.”

He continued, “Most people never notice them. But they add up to a real relationship, because that’s what diabetes is. It’s an ongoing conversation between your body, your mind and your devices.”

The Jonas Brothers star, who is now a father, shared that the “unpredictability” of diabetes “threw” him when he initially received his diagnosis in 2005 at the age of 13. (Jonas and wife Priyanka Chopra Jonas welcomed their daughter, Malti, via surrogate in 2022.)

“I’d built my whole life around this unrealistic structure and belief that I was in control. You took that apart over the years, bit by bit,” he wrote. “Part of me is grateful I learned that lesson. Part of me resents you for it. Full stop. There are days I wonder what life would feel like without you.”

Despite resenting the disease for its impact on his life, Jonas also reflected on the positives of learning to live with Type 1 diabetes.

“I always struggled to feel like I belonged anywhere. At school, I felt misunderstood. In most circles, for that matter. Through my childhood and youth, that was the theme. It wasn’t until I started talking openly about life with type 1, and meeting other people who were living it too, that I finally felt like I belonged somewhere,” he explained, adding that he never wanted to be seen as a “victim.”

From Jonas’ perspective, his candidness about this journey may help others see someone like him “out there living their dreams.”

“I share my story for the 13-year-old who just got diagnosed and is lying in a hospital bed, scared to death. I want them to be able to look up and see someone out there living their dreams while managing all of this, every single day,” he said. “So thank you, old friend, for the lessons I never asked for. I’m going to keep sharing my story and telling the truth, so someone out there feels a little less alone.”

Assessing if Avalanche’s growing network activity can spur AVAX’s price recovery

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Avalanche sits among the top-30 cryptos by market cap with a value of close to $3.64 billion. It’s in the news today after it crossed the 1.60 billion mark for total transactions on the mainnet since launching in September 2020. This new milestone was achieved despite a bearish crypto market.

However, that’s not the entire story. Especially since the three-month average for transactions seemed to be far below the volume it commanded in its initial days.

At press time, the number was well below 1 million, while the volume 6 years ago when it launched was above 3 million.

Worth noting, however, that in the last 24 hours, its network fees spiked by 30%, indicating network congestion. Hence, the question: What’s behind the uptick in the number of transactions?

On-chain data from CoinGlass revealed that AVAX traders have been defying the market downtrend. Both Spot and Futures markets were net buying at 51.60%, with a bullish divergence confirmed on the 15-minute chart.

The Futures CVD was at $2.39 million, while that of Spot was at $462K. This implied that the derivatives market was more optimistic than the spot one.

These traders could be buying the dip since AVAX has been on a downtrend over the past week. In fact, it fell from around $12 to $8, a loss equivalent to about 32%.

At press time, its mindshare was also down 10%, with sentiment from top influencers turning neutral.

The existing optimism could be attributed to the ecosystem growth, especially on the real-world front. It’s growing exponentially on a quarter-on-quarter basis, especially when the third and second quarters were compared.

For instance, Avalanche’s DEX volume doubled to about $5.33 billion, indicating heightened trading activity both on buys and sells.

Additionally, RWAs on C-Chain reached $1.22 billion, with the tokenized treasuries leading with a growth of 14% to reach $545 million. At the same time, Dinari stock purchases jumped by over 34%, equivalent to $9 million. These figures confirmed AVAX’s growing RWA financial activity.

Finally, card spending surged by 74%, which totaled more than $10 million. Similarly, on-chain credit grew by 34%, reaching $37.40 million. Both implied that traders have been using Avalanche for payments.

Despite such strong activity, if the higher high at $7.90 is lost as support, AVAX could slide to $6.85 or subsequently test the psychological level at $6. However, if capital flows into the chain through RWA, the downside risk could be managed.

'Zlatan Ibrahimovic Ended My England Career in 10 Minutes - I Never Played Again'

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

While plenty of attention is devoted to Zlatan Ibrahimovic's character and his larger-than-life personality, his ego wouldn't have been nearly as effective if he hadn't also been one of the best players on the planet during the prime of his career. The Swede was a phenomenon in front of goal, ranking among the greatest strikers in football history, and wherever he played, he rarely struggled to find the back of the net.

Ibrahimovic represented some of the biggest teams in world football throughout his career and ranks among the most decorated footballers of all time when it comes to trophies won. He played for Barcelona, Inter Milan, AC Milan, Paris Saint-Germain and Manchester United, alongside several other notable clubs. His imposing frame was matched by remarkable agility and speed, meaning there were few defenders in the beautiful game capable of keeping him quiet for an entire match. Having retired in 2023, the striker finished with 949 appearances for club and country, scoring 558 goals in the process.

Facing the superstar during his prime could have serious consequences for a player's career too, as one England centre-back discovered when his international career was effectively brought to an end after going up against Ibrahimovic.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic says his former Manchester United teammate deserves credit for Cristiano Ronaldo’s success at Old Trafford.

After several seasons impressing for Stoke City in the Premier League, Ryan Shawcross received his first-ever call-up to the England team in 2012. Supporters had been pushing for the defender to get an opportunity with the national side for some time and he was finally handed his chance, but his debut came against Sweden. He was tasked with trying to deal with Ibrahimovic and, by his own admission, things didn't go according to plan. In fact, the incident turned him into a source of jokes within his family. Via quotes shared by the Telegraph, Shawcross said:

"I’m the butt of the joke in my family, just because the 10 minutes I had in international football was ruined by one man. They are consistently saying that Zlatan ruined my career! I had a chance and I don’t think many other defenders have stopped him. I was given 10 minutes to show what I could do and probably didn’t provide a good enough performance. I was against one of the best strikers in the world who was on a hot streak at the time and these things happen."

The centre-back was able to look back on the episode with good humour at the time, but he never earned another England appearance for the rest of his career. That was unfortunate, considering he enjoyed a strong spell in the Premier League and had arguably done enough to deserve another opportunity.

Zlatan Ibrahimovic previously had tattoos on his body that are no longer there - here's the story behind it.

He never managed to join one of England's biggest clubs, but for much of his career, Shawcross was a top-level defender who held his own against some of the Premier League's finest strikers. After progressing through the Manchester United academy, he spent time on loan with Royal Antwerp and Stoke before eventually making his move to the latter permanent. The Potters were playing in the Championship at the time, but they soon secured promotion to the top flight with Shawcross at the centre of their defence.

From there, he helped Stoke establish themselves as a midtable Premier League side, with the club remaining in the top flight for 10 seasons before eventually being relegated to the Championship. Throughout his Premier League spell, Shawcross was highly impressive and attracted interest from several clubs over the years. No move ultimately materialised, however, and he stayed with Stoke for 13 years.

Across that period, the centre-back made 453 appearances for the Potters, including 317 in the top flight. He was a hugely important servant to the club, but departed in 2021 to join Inter Miami. His stay there lasted just one season before he retired, and he never represented England again. It's fair to say that Ibrahimovic may have played a role in that.

Man City Signed the ‘English Messi’ - He Made 3 Appearances in 6 Years

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Eleven years after being touted as the 'English Lionel Messi' and subsequently being signed by Manchester City for £12 million, Patrick Roberts is plying his trade in the Championship for Birmingham City.

Born in Kingston upon Thames in south-west London, Roberts joined Fulham's academy at the age of 13. After making his debut for the Cottagers at 17, City opted to sign the wide man for £12 million in the summer of 2015.

He went on to make three appearances for the Citizens' first team, before a succession of underwhelming loan spells saw him sold to Sunderland in 2022. After four years on Wearside, Roberts swapped the Premier League for the second tier again, joining Birmingham permanently in the summer.

He's now an important figure in Chris Davies' team, who are vying for promotion back to the big time. From being tipped to lead City to major honours to being compared to Messi, it all could have been so different for Roberts.

After making waves in Fulham's academy, Felix Magath called Roberts up to a matchday squad for a Premier League fixture in March 2014, when the winger was just 17. He'd just signed his first professional contract, and would go on to make his senior debut a week later against the club he'd eventually sign for, Manchester City.

Introduced as a 55th-minute substitute against the north-west outfit, Magath showered the then teenager with praise after this surprise inclusion, describing the player as an 'extraordinary talent' (via the Mirror).

Naturally, these complimentary words sparked excitement amongst Fulham fans about the level of talent they might have on their hands.

Roberts would go on to make 17 appearances for the West Londoners in the Championship in the following season. Despite failing to score in any of these outings, City bought into the hype around the starlet, forking out £12 million to bring him to the Etihad in the summer of 2015.

It was at this point that Roberts' nickname in the Fulham academy became common knowledge. His peers in the youth ranks at Craven Cottage had called him 'Mini Messi', which led to many branding him the 'English Messi' when he signed for the Citizens (via the Mirror).

As well as being tipped for greatness by Messi, Roberts himself referenced his likeness to the Argentine, citing the legendary playmaker as his idol in February 2016 (via PlanetFootball).

"It’s a conscious thing for me to be like Messi," he said.

"I’ve watched him for years and he’s my idol. You try to copy what he does, and it also comes naturally to me as well. Because I’m left-footed, I tend to move the same way as he does. In games, I try to replicate what he does. I’ve studied him on YouTube and when La Liga is on I watch that all the time. My view is that you watch the best to learn from the best."

It's fair to say that Roberts didn't live up to this tag. After spending six months on the periphery of Manuel Pellegrini's squad, making just three appearances for the Premier League giants, he was shipped out to Celtic on loan.

This sparked a series of temporary stints, which lasted seven years and included stops at Girona, Norwich City, Middlesbrough, Derby County and Troyes, before he finally parted ways with City in 2022.

While Roberts hasn't lived up to the potential he once promised, it would be unfair to dismiss his career as a failure. Most players who are compared to some of the game's greatest players when they are young don't get close to replicating their idol's accomplishments, and the ex-City prodigy has certainly achieved a lot more than most.

Finally settling at Sunderland in 2022, after enjoying an impressive loan spell with the Black Cats in the back half of the 2021/22 campaign, Roberts shone for the north-east club for several years in the Championship.

Lamine Yamal may have won, but some former Ballon d'Or winners didn't even include him in their top three.

He made 147 appearances on Wearside, playing a key role in their promotion charge in the 2024/25 season. The former wonderkid was then granted a solitary Premier League appearance for Regis Le Bris' side before making the switch to Birmingham.

Since joining the Blues, he's scored four goals and established himself as a core member in an improving team that is now looking to return to the top flight for the first time in 15 years.

Patrick Stewart's '90s Fantasy Movie With Neve Campbell Is Streaming For Free

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

An adaptation of Oscar Wilde's 1887 short story, Sydney Macartney's "The Canterville Ghost" aired on ABC on January 27, 1996, and, perhaps surprisingly, featured notable central roles for both the late legend Sir Patrick Stewart and Neve Campbell. Indeed, 1996 was a banner year for both actors, with Stewart appearing in the film "Star Trek: First Contact" and Campbell headlining two of the best teen movies of the 1990s over the same 12-month period (namely, "The Craft" and "Scream"). Stewart, of course, was already riding high professionally by that point. But for Campbell, this was the final vault before proper stardom following her breakout role on the hit series "Party of Five."

It should be noted that Wilde's original story is a light tale about a haunted mansion, and Macartney's TV movie — which is currently streaming for free on platforms like Tubi and Prime Video — affects a similarly lighthearted tone. Wilde's source material concerns the Otises, a family of two parents and three children who move into Canterville Castle, despite warnings that it is haunted. Sure enough, its ghost tries to frighten them off, but the Otises react with mere annoyance. For example, when Mrs. Otis spots a bloodstain on the carpet, she dryly states, "I don't at all care for blood-stains in a sitting-room."

Macartney's adaptation transposes the action to the modern day. In the movie, the Otises inevitably have to deal with the specter of Sir Simon de Canterville, played by Stewart. Campbell, meanwhile, plays the young Virginia Otis, and the story is told largely from her perspective. As for Sir Simon, he eventually reveals the trauma that left him in his ghostly state, which has to do with his beloved dead wife, Eleanor.

The TV movie version of "The Canterville Ghost" adds a few fun flourishes to the story. The Otis family patriarch, Hiram, is played by Edward Wiley, and there comes a scene where Virginia and the Ghost, having begun communicating, have to convince Hiram that ghosts are real. In a nod to Sir Patrick Stewart's past with the Royal Shakespeare Company, Virginia and the Ghost get Hiram's attention by staging a scene from "Hamlet" wherein Sir Simon plays the Ghost of Hamlet's Father. Fittingly enough, Stewart would later portray both the Ghost of Hamlet's Father and Claudius in a celebrated 2008 BBC production of the play opposite David Tennant.

Initially, as mentioned, Sir Simon is determined to scare away the Otis family to remain in his sad, ghostly state. Soon after moving in, however, Virginia finds a secret passageway inside the Canterville Castle leading to a chamber where Sir Simon hangs out during the daylight hours. Soon, she and her brothers befriend Sir Simon, and we learn that, despite his intense demeanor, he isn't so terrible or scary. (Virginia's brothers even startle Sir Simon with a fake ghost puppet they construct themselves.) At the same time, Virginia develops a crush on Francis, the Duke of Cheshire (Daniel Betts), a local nobleman who plays a larger role here than he does in Oscar Wilde's original story.

The central drama arises when Hiram believes Virginia is responsible for Sir Simon's shenanigans and threatens to send her back home to the U.S., where she won't be able to flirt with Francis anymore. This is basically her motivation for proving the ghost is real because, again, "The Canterville Ghost" is a fairly fanciful yarn.

There aren't a lot of professional reviews of the "Canterville Ghost" TV movie readily available. One can find a write-up on Common Sense Media by Brian Costello, who gave the film a high rating (which indicates that it's perfectly okay for younger kids). Costello also praised Patrick Stewart, writing that he brings a Shakespearean relish to his character. "[H]is performance," the review reads, "more than makes up for the dated special effects."

David Saunderson, writing for the British/Irish horror website Spooky Isles, had some mild complaints about how the TV movie strays from Oscar Wilde's original story. With weary resignation, he observed that Otises had to be made American for American audiences, despite being British in Wilde's source material. He added that the movie isn't as good as the 1944 film adaptation starring Charles Laughton in the title role and that he found the modern contrivances to be a little tiresome, despite Stewart's solid work. As he put it:

"Patrick Stewart is a fine actor, and fine actors make fine Canterville Ghosts — he's very dramatic, a bit scary, and works well with Neve Campbell [...] who plays the Otis daughter who hates that she has been brought to England but soon befriends the ghost and learns from the experience — blah, blah, blah — usual teenage angst in TV movie!"

Notably, 1996's "The Canterville Ghost" won an Emmy for Outstanding Individual Achievement in Music Composition for a Miniseries or Special, which is no small feat. Stream it now, and see what you make of it.

Opinion: Liverpool handed major transfer boost in chase for £45m sensation

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Liverpool were probably expecting their squad to be completed by now.

They've spent over £600 million on new players across the last couple of seasons and, if we're being honest, things still aren't great.

Multiple areas of of Andoni Iraola's squad have failed to meet the standards expected of them and have forced fans to question whether some of the players that their team have signed were even worth buying in the first place.

This thought process has led to different questions being asked about their first-team squad and has caused many players to be linked with a move.

And now, the Anfield outfit seem to have just been handed a major boost in their pursuit for one of their main targets.

Barcelona want to sign Levi Colwill which could open the door for a Ronald Araujo transfer

Ronald Araujo has arguably been one of Liverpool's best signings this season.

The Uruguayan was brought in on a season-long loan with the Reds having an option to buy worth €55 million (£45.9 million).

This may not seem like all that much money with how good the centre-back has been but, since he'll be 28 years old by the time Liverpool can activate their option to buy, it's unclear whether he'll be worth them signing or not.

Obviously, things such as his injury history also need to be taken into consideration.

But, luckily for Liverpool, a fresh update from Caught Offside has shared that Barcelona now want to sign Levi Colwill from Chelsea.

"Barcelona view Colwill as a top target after studying his performances and stats in great detail, there have been internal discussions about putting a bid together, possibly as early as this January," they wrote.

Levi Colwill could be Barcelona's replacement for Ronald Araujo

With how happy Araujo seems to be at Liverpool and how happy the Reds' fanbase seems to be with the Uruguayan, a permanent deal for him makes sense.

His ability to play as a right-back or a centre-back is massive with both of those areas major problems for Liverpool at the moment.

In fact, their whole defence could do with a re-jig.

Barca are likely to stick to their price of €55 million for Araujo but, if they manage to get a deal over the line for Colwill before then, they could become a little more lenient.

Araujo's experience is unmatched compared to some of the other players that Liverpool have been linked with.

But, only time will tell whether the Reds feel like a move for the 27-year-old is worth it.

Apps to distract you from the endless cycle of doomscrolling

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

You open your phone to check the time or a quick message. The next thing you know, an hour has passed and you’ve scrolled through endless celebrity drama, cat videos, awful news stories, influencer rants, and whatever else the algorithm decided to throw at you.

Even though you probably don’t want to keep wasting your time and energy on this mind-numbing content, you do it again the next day.

Doomscrolling, the habit of spending excessive amounts of time consuming content on social media, has become incredibly widespread. A survey from last year found that 64% of Americans say they doomscroll.

Researchers have warned that doomscrolling can negatively affect several aspects of your well-being, including your mental health and attention span. Spending long periods scrolling can lead to brain fatigue, difficulty focusing, and disrupted sleep. And if a lot of the content you’re consuming is negative or stressful, it can leave you feeling disheartened, anxious, and emotionally drained.

It’s hard to break the cycle of doomscrolling, but there are plenty of apps that provide content that’s engaging and productive.

Of course, you could always read a book or go for a walk (we have a guide on how to stop doomscrolling), but this list is for when you have a few spare minutes and want something to do on your phone that isn’t endless scrolling.

Finch is a self-care pet app that’s designed to help you build healthy habits. You care for a cute virtual pet by completing small daily goals, such as drinking enough water, going on a walk, and practicing mindfulness. You can write about your day, complete a breathing exercise, or send good vibes to a friend by adding them to the app.

The more self-care you do, the more your pet thrives. The app offers a fun way to trade mindless scrolling for small moments of self-care.

Finch is available for free on iOS and Android.

Libby is a free reading app that connects you to your local public library’s digital collection of ebooks, audiobooks, and magazines. All you need is a library card to borrow thousands of titles, which makes it easy to turn screen time into something productive.

You can explore a new book during a commute or while waiting at a doctor’s office, turning moments when your fingers might otherwise itch to scroll into opportunities to read.

Libby is available for free on iOS and Android.

If you want to take a break from doomscrolling and try something creative, Dudel Draw might be a good app for you. Each day, the app gives you a random shape that you turn into a drawing. These daily shapes vary from basic geometrical forms to more complex and abstract designs.

You can also get your friends in on the creativity with some fun competition by comparing your different creations, which could provide a nice break from sending each other endless TikToks or Instagram Reels.

If you still want to feel connected to the world without scrolling through social media, you can check out Radio Garden. The app lets you listen to over 25,000 live radio stations from across the globe.

Once you open the app, you’ll see green dots marking cities and towns. Tap any dot to listen to radio stations broadcasting from that location. You can add favorites or search for radio stations, countries, and places.

Radio Garden is free, but it also offers an ad-free premium plan for $2.99 per month. The app is available on both iOS and Android.

Elevate is an app designed to help improve focus, memory, reading, math skills, and other cognitive abilities. It includes over 40 games to train different abilities you use in everyday life, from reading faster to comparing prices more efficiently.

You can track training streaks and compare your mind’s performance over time.

The app offers a free version with access to three games per day, or a yearly subscription of $39.99 for unlimited access. It’s available on both iOS and Android.

Vocabulary is an app that helps you learn new words every day. You can pick your difficulty level and choose categories that interest you, like emotions, the human body, business, and more. Each word comes with a definition, example sentences, and a guide on how to pronounce it.

The app also includes mini-games to help you review the words you’ve learned. You can set a goal of how many words you want to learn each week and create a regular learning routine.

Vocabulary offers a free trial, and then costs $4.99 per month or $59.99 per year. It’s available on iOS and Android.

If you’re a geography nerd looking to test your knowledge or just want to improve your geography skills, Seterra is perfect for you. The app features over 300 different games to test your map skills. You can test your knowledge of world flags; discover oceans, seas, and rivers; and explore mountain ranges and volcanoes across the globe.

Seterra lets you track your progress across categories and see leaderboards for top scorers for each game.

The app is free and available on both iOS and Android.

The NYT Games app offers several word, logic, and number games that change every day to exercise your mind. You can play the crossword, try the word-guessing game Wordle, group words with a common theme in Connections, see how many words you can make from seven letters in Spelling Bee, and more.

The app costs $5.99 per month for unlimited access and archives, but some games like Wordle, Strands, and the Mini Crossword are available to play for free.

NYT Games is available on both iOS and Android.

If you want to learn a new language but want to try something other than Duolingo, Drops is a good option. The app uses visually engaging mini‑games to help you build vocabulary and common phrases in more than 45 languages, with bite‑sized lessons designed to be completed in about five minutes a day.

Drops is designed for both beginners and experts who want to grow their foreign language vocabulary.

The free version of the app offers five-minute lessons per day. You can get unlimited access and premium features for $11.99 per month or $79.99 per year. The app is available on both iOS and Android.

Serie A | Fiorentina 2-2 Juventus: Old Lady fight back in Florence thriller

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Juventus fought back twice with Nick Woltemade and Gleison Bremer to grab a point away to Fiorentina, who had been leading through Mateo Pellegrino and Alieu Njie.

Luciano Spalletti hoped the Juve crisis was behind him after back-to-back wins over Napoli and AZ Alkmaar, despite a growing injury crisis. Weston McKennie and Zeki Celik returned to the squad, with Kenan Yildiz, Andrea Cambiaso, Nico Gonzalez, Manuel Locatelli, Khephren Thuram and Kamil Grabara out, but a new trident was launched with Jeremie Boga, Nick Woltemade and Randal Kolo Muani.

The Viola finally got themselves back on track with a 4-2 victory away to Sassuolo on Monday, after scraping two points from five rounds. Marin Pongracic, Luca Ranieri, Fabiano Parisi and Arthur Atta were still on the treatment table, but Pedro Goncalves and Christ Inao Oulai were preferred to Nicolò Fagioli.

It was a lightning start to the game, as Pedro Goncalves floated a corner kick to the back post for the Mateo Pellegrino free header to give Fiorentina the early lead. The Argentine pulled out the old Gabriel Batistuta celebration in front of the home fans.

McKennie tried to replicate it at the other end with a header inches wide from a corner, then David De Gea scrambled across for a one-handed save on the Juan Cabal chest and volley from the edge of the penalty area.

Juventus did equalise when McKennie sent Randal Kolo Muani down the right, he turned Viery inside out to roll across for Woltemade to sweep in first-time from 10 yards with the inside of the right boot. After three goals in the Europa League, this was his first in Serie A.

Kolo Muani was wreaking havoc down the right against Viery, again pulling back for Woltemade, but this time De Gea made a big save and Teun Koopmeiners’ follow-up was deflected wide.

Cabal also stung De Gea’s gloves from distance and a Kolo Muani effort was charged down in the box by a defender whose hands were clamped behind his back, while Cher Ndour drilled wide and Pedro Goncalves failed to make the most of Jhon Lucumi error playing out from defence, as he too slipped at the crucial moment.

Fiorentina restored their advantage when Alex Jimenez saw his low cross-shot from the right palmed into a crowd by Guglielmo Vicario, with Alieu Njie quicker to react than the Juve defenders to bundle over the line. It was the Swede’s third consecutive game on target.

Franco Mastantuono nearly added another moments later, cutting inside to drill just wide of the far post, but Zeki Celik came off the bench and immediately pulled back from the by-line with no teammates ready to finish it off, then Douglas Luiz couldn’t keep the header down on an Edon Zhegrova cross.

Juventus clawed their way back into it again from a Douglas Luiz corner, was De Gea was hesitant and Gleison Bremer anticipated Radu Dragusin to turn in from close range with a combination of head and shoulder.

Penalty appeals were rejected for an awkward bouncing ball that struck Joao Mario on the arm, while Fiorentina were also angered when Dragusin nodded in a Mandragora corner at the 82nd minute, but it was disallowed for his push on Bremer.

Marco Brescianini’s scorcher in stoppages was parried by Vicario, with Bremer clearing before Willie Gnonto could pounce, then the Juve goalkeeper also kept out Mastantuono’s solo effort at the base of the near post.

‘I expect to move forward’: Carrick denies risk of Manchester United sack

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

‘Is there?’ manager retorts when told of talk over position

Bizarre 4-4 draw with Villa leaves United in bottom three

A tetchy Michael Carrick dismissed a suggestion he could resign as head coach for the good of the team after Sunday’s chaotic 4-4 draw with Aston Villa left Manchester United in the relegation zone heading into the international window.

Carrick’s side sit in 18th place with eight points from 10 games and have won only once, in August at home to Ipswich, in the league this season. It is now a club record eight Premier League games without a win.His job remains in the balance due to the dismal run of form.

Carrick was asked on Sky Sports if he was considering his position. “I’m not,” said the 45-year-old, who was curt when the interviewer said that it was clear there would be conversations regarding his future before United’s next game, which is away to Liverpool on 22 November.

“Is there?” he replied. “You’re telling me. I’m quite clear with what direction the club needs to go in, and it’s got to win games. We haven’t won enough games, but I expect to move forward and keep working on the things that we’ve been working on and showing a lot of the good in the game today, tighten up on a few mistakes and win football matches.”

In the Guardian app, tap the Profile settings button at the top right, then select Notifications. Turn on sport notifications.

If you already have the Guardian app, make sure you’re on the most recent version.

If you don't have the Guardian app, download it from the iOS App Store on iPhone or the Google Play store on Android by searching for 'The Guardian'.

Carrick insisted he is seeing enough in United’s play. “Yeah, 100%. I think there’s more than enough,” he said. “Goals change games and you’ve got to deal with it, and it’s part of something we have to improve on drastically, but there’s more than enough. We’re just making it too difficult for ourselves at the moment in certain times.”

Villa scored after 59 seconds through Alysson before Nicolas Jackson gave them a 2-0 lead after just four minutes – with goalkeeper Senne Lammens culpable for both goals.

Despite Bruno Fernandes, from the penalty spot, and Youri Tielemans pulling United back all square by the break, further mistakes from Diogo Dalot and Leny Yoro allowed John McGinn and Leon Goretzka to make it 4-2 to Villa. Dalot’s clumsy foul on Alysson conceded a penalty that McGinn converted and Yoro’s header towards his own goal led to Villa’s messy fourth for Goretzka.

In stoppage time, goals from Joshua Zirkzee and Patrick Dorgu rescued an unlikely point for United.

Kick off your evenings with the Guardian's take on the world of football

When Dalot was replaced in the second half, the makeshift left-back was jeered by some United fans. The manager was asked if he could understand the supporters’ reaction. Carrick said: “We’ve got to be careful with that, I understand the frustration of the supporters, so I get it. We’re all frustrated, we’re all disappointed, and the players are as much as anyone, I guarantee you that. Diogo, he gives absolutely everything.”

Unai Emery was also left upset, Villa’s manager suggesting that Dorgu’s 96th-minute headed equaliser should not have stood as Lammens, up for a last-gasp corner, knocked into his opposite number, Zion Suzuki. Emery said: “How we lost [our way] the last six minutes at the time, conceding two goals is very frustrating. The keeper, as well, is adapting but he still has work to do and how we have to defend when they are blocking us. In another country, maybe it’s a foul but here it’s not.

“I am disappointed but we move on. I have to analyse this match. It is very important to analyse deeply. First half, we started very good, but they were pushing. We were resilient. We tried to change. We started very well, scoring two goals again.”

Ezra Dyer: Maybe the Saturn Sky Red Line Is Not Quite a Discount Viper, but It Comes Close

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

This car is proof that the old GM’s ideas weren’t all bad, as I find out when I take custody of one.

From the November/December 2026 issue of Car and Driver.

Back in 2007, I gave my father-in-law, Roger, a ride in a Dodge Viper test car. He loved it, and I offhandedly said, “If you like this, a Saturn Sky Red Line is 80 percent of the performance for 30 percent of the price.” That was the extent of my salesmanship, so I was surprised a month or so later to learn that he’d gone out and bought a new Sky Red Line—for less than $30,000.

I mean, people ask me for car-buying advice all the time. But the way it usually works is that someone asks my pick for an electric sedan, I tell them to drive a Lucid Air, and then they buy an F-350 Power Stroke. So Roger’s follow-through was both rewarding and unnerving, because now he owned a Sky, and I owned my opinion, which is dangerous. I once had a boss who asked me to name a car that was “overpowered but understated,” and I blurted three words that I came to regret: “Volkswagen Phaeton W-12.” I think soon thereafter the company handbook was amended to make Phaeton recommendations a fireable HR violation.

See all results for used cars for sale near 95864

Fortunately, Roger liked the Sky, the first new GM car he’d purchased since a 1982 Camaro Berlinetta that was so terrible that he swore he’d never buy another GM vehicle. He usually sticks to such decrees, but maybe Saturn got a pass because the brand wasn’t around when his Chevy was putting him on a first-name basis with the local tow-truck dispatchers. In any case, the only problem the Sky ever had pertained to the notorious GM ignition-switch recall, wherein pre-bankruptcy GM’s pathological commitment to cheapness led to ignition cylinders unfit for a Little Tikes Cozy Coupe and thus occasional random engine shutdowns. This happened to Roger once on a back road, but he was unbothered. “I just put it in neutral, restarted it, and kept driving,” he told me. “Didn’t even stop.” I guess the Camaro might have permanently recalibrated his expectations for build quality.

Eventually, Roger stopped driving, but he still liked the Sky, so it sat in his driveway long enough for the neighbors to plant hedges so they wouldn’t have to look at it. But where they saw a dented, pollen-crusted car with two flat tires, I saw a dented, pollen-crusted car with two flat tires and a 260-hp turbo four, a stick shift, rear-wheel drive, and a limited-slip differential. Roger said that if I could get it going, I could have it for the bargain price of “get it out of my driveway.”

So, one day, I arrived with a battery jump pack, an air compressor, and five gallons of fresh gas. The questionable tires held air, and the electrics powered up. I splashed in some fuel and turned the key. The Sky fired up like it had been parked an hour before, its digital odometer showing just 43,000 miles. I put it in neutral, let out the clutch, and it immediately stalled because it wasn’t actually in neutral. Turns out you can wiggle the Red Line shifter side to side quite a bit even when it’s in second gear.

See all results for used cars for sale near 95864

Other revelations mostly pertained to ran-when-parked status. The flat-spotted tires and various wheel-on-curb encounters gave the Sky the ride and directional stability of a grocery cart you pull out of the corral and immediately swap for a different one. The battery wouldn’t hold a charge, the interior smelled like a cologne called Old Must, and the A/C didn’t blow cold. But the scrappy Red Line ran like a champ, the turbo issuing a jaunty chirp on upshifts and all 260 horses feeling ready to gallop. It just needed a little deferred maintenance.

I bought some Pirelli P Zeros and got an alignment, since I’ve found that cars drive better with round tires all pointed in the same direction. I changed the oil and recharged the A/C. Next, I tackled the battery, which is mounted somewhere near Earth’s molten core. Accessing it requires removing the right front fender, and I got impatient and only mostly unbolted it, figuring I could bend the flexible composite panel enough to get the battery out. That’s how I tore a fender while replacing the battery, which is not a sequence of words I expected to write back when I told Roger that a Sky Red Line is a discount Viper.

But I stand by my assessment. The Sky was, and is, huge fun. It’s agile and powerful, low-slung and eager to carve a corner. It makes any drive feel like Bowser and Koopa Troopa should be trying to beat you to the finish line of Rainbow Road. The critiques that dogged the Sky when it was new—cheap interior, little storage space, poor visibility—seem less important now. Saturn ran out of time to perfect the Sky, but it’s perfect enough. Dents and all.

Ezra Dyer is a Car and Driver senior editor and columnist. He's now based in North Carolina but still remembers how to turn right. He owns a 2009 GEM e4 and once drove 206 mph. Those facts are mutually exclusive.

“And as a manager or as a coach, it’s a nightmare when you don’t know what the performance level your team’s going to give you. I need consistency in performances. We need consistency as a club. That isn’t consistent, so I’ve got to work out what we can do with it.”

XRP could double while this yield strategy leaves you far behind

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

In a modeled doubling of XRP, a $10,000 XRP–RLUSD pool position reaches about $14,142 versus $20,000 from simply holding XRP, before fees and costs.

The XRP Ledger pool pairing XRP with Ripple’s dollar stablecoin, RLUSD, quotes about 4.7% annualized trading-fee yield. Joining this native pool changes what an XRP holder owns. The position becomes a shifting mix of XRP and the dollar stablecoin, surrendering some exposure to a rebound while cushioning some of a decline.

The trade matters with XRP near $1.16, down 7.1% over seven days in CryptoSlate’s November 7 capture. A hypothetical $10,000 pool position would accrue about $9 in fees inside the liquidity position over one week at the displayed rate if prices stayed unchanged, before costs. That is the scale of income to compare with the portfolio’s changing exposure.

At 13:07 UTC on November 7, XRPL.to’s pool data displayed a 4.7% annualized seven-day fee estimate and 0.09% weekly yield. The identified pool held approximately 1.94 million XRP and 2.25 million RLUSD. Data by xrpl.to.

The specific pool is rhWTXC2m2gGGA9WozUaoMm6kLAVPb1tcS3; its RLUSD issuer is rMxCKbEDwqr76QuheSUMdEGf4B9xJ8m5De, matching Ripple’s official address.

XRPL.to reported about 1.14 million XRP-equivalent of seven-day trading volume and 3,502 XRP-equivalent in estimated fees. Its API guide describes the annualized rate as recent trading fees relative to liquidity, scaled to a year. The quoted income varies with trading and does not lock in a year’s earnings.

Those figures are estimates, not a reconstruction of fees actually earned throughout the week. The displayed fee figure exactly matches seven-day volume multiplied by the current 0.308% trading fee, yet the response records a fee update on November 5, inside that week. Without the earlier rates and transaction-level fees, it does not establish realized income. XRPL also allows eligible auction-slot traders to pay discounted fees.

Under the XRPL’s constant-product mechanics, trading changes the XRP and RLUSD quantities represented by liquidity-provider tokens. Fees accumulate inside the pool and become part of the assets those LP tokens can redeem.

Two comparisons are therefore needed. Holding everything in XRP preserves full XRP price exposure. Leaving an initial 50/50 XRP–RLUSD allocation untouched starts with the same asset mix as the pool but avoids its automatic rebalancing.

Consider equal $10,000 starting investments. In a hypothetical model with RLUSD fixed at $1, no fees or costs, and the pool adjusting to XRP’s new relative price, a 20% XRP rise leaves the pool worth about $10,954. Untouched 50/50 holdings reach $11,000; an all-XRP holding reaches $12,000.

Most of the gap to all-XRP comes from choosing stablecoin exposure in the first place. The smaller gap to untouched 50/50 holdings isolates the rebalancing shortfall commonly called impermanent loss.

The downside comparison changes the conclusion. With XRP down 20%, the modeled pool is worth about $8,944, versus $9,000 for untouched 50/50 holdings and $8,000 for all-XRP. The pool still loses money, but retains more value than the fully exposed XRP position.

Larger moves widen the rebalancing gap. Doubling XRP leaves the modeled pool at $14,142 against $15,000 for untouched 50/50 and $20,000 for all-XRP. Halving XRP leaves $7,071, $7,500 and $5,000, respectively. These are scenarios, not forecasts or measured returns.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Each strategy starts with $10,000. Compare hypothetical XRP moves while holding RLUSD at $1.

The pool rises, but trails both holding benchmarks. Most of the all-XRP gap comes from starting with stablecoin exposure.

The pool loses dollar value, but retains more than all-XRP. It remains below untouched 50/50 holdings.

A doubling widens the upside surrendered. The pool and untouched 50/50 both started with half their capital in RLUSD.

A halving leaves the pool ahead of all-XRP in dollar value, yet behind untouched 50/50 holdings.

The pool cushions some XRP downside and gives up upside. Untouched 50/50 holdings isolate its rebalancing shortfall.

Scenarios are not forecasts or realized returns. Excludes trading fees, costs, slippage and peg movement. No time horizon or fee-income prediction. The pool's quantity changes are not new investor buying.

Hypothetical equal starting value V=$10,000; r is XRP's ending/start price. All XRP=Vr; untouched 50/50=V(1+r)/2; constant-product pool=V√r. RLUSD=$1; arbitrage aligns the pool to price. Values rounded to whole dollars. Outcome bars share a zero-to-$20,000 scale in every state; starting strips show initial capital allocation. Gaps use unrounded values.

Scenarios are not forecasts or realized returns. Excludes trading fees, costs, slippage and peg movement. No time horizon or fee-income prediction. The pool's quantity changes are not new investor buying.

One week’s quoted 0.09% is about $9 on $10,000 at unchanged prices. If that percentage is instead applied as a hypothetical uplift to the pool’s value after a price move, it offsets the modeled shortfall against untouched 50/50 holdings only to roughly an 8.1% XRP fall or an 8.9% rise.

That calculation holds the fee uplift constant while changing XRP’s price. Actual fees depend on trading and the path taken. The range is neither a capital-loss buffer nor a break-even comparison with all-XRP.

RLUSD adds separate risks. Ripple’s terms permit blacklisting and freezing under legal or compliance conditions; assuming a $1 price does not remove issuer or redemption risk.

The pool commits native XRP as trading inventory. A provider’s actual return depends on how much XRP and RLUSD each LP token can redeem, and their combined value over a matched holding period. Comparing that return after costs with both holding benchmarks over the same period can show whether the fees compensated for the exposure surrendered.

Also known as "Akiba," Liam Wright is a reporter, podcast producer, and Editor-in-Chief at CryptoSlate. He believes that decentralized technology has the potential to make…

Report: Liverpool to beat Barcelona to £75m Virgil van Dijk replacement

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Virgil van Dijk is undoubtedly one of the best defenders in history, but his time at Anfield cannot last forever.

The legendary Liverpool captain is into the final 12 months of his deal, and as things stand will be leaving Merseyside for free in the summer.

At 35-years-old, the Dutchman has played every minute of the Reds' Premier League campaign so far, in what could be his final season at Anfield.

Julian Ward and Liverpool's recruitment team have already begun planning for a potential departure, and one star is now reportedly being lined up for a massive £75 million move.

Barcelona to battle Liverpool for Premier League defenderSeveral of Europe's top defenders have been identified as potential replacements for Liverpool's skipper, but one move now gaining traction is for Chelsea's Levi Colwill. The 23-year-old has started all but one of the Blues' Premier League games this season, captaining his side on three occasions. Now, the Reds are lining up a move that could cost as much as £75 million to bring the five-capped England international to Merseyside.

Several of Europe's top defenders have been identified as potential replacements for Liverpool's skipper, but one move now gaining traction is for Chelsea's Levi Colwill.

The 23-year-old has started all but one of the Blues' Premier League games this season, captaining his side on three occasions.

Now, the Reds are lining up a move that could cost as much as £75 million to bring the five-capped England international to Merseyside.

That's according to a report from Caught Offside, which claims that Liverpool hold a strong interest in the defender and will try and convince him to consider a new challenge.

The report states that Chelsea want to keep Colwill at Stamford Bridge but that they could consider a bid of between £65-75 million.

Liverpool aren't the only team interested in the 23-year-old however, with La Liga leaders Barcelona also monitoring the situation.

Chelsea would likely be more willing to deal with a team in another league rather than selling one of their best players to a direct rival, but if the Reds can make an attractive enough offer then they may be able to land their man.

Colwill has been on the radar at Anfield for some time, and Liverpool could finally act on their long-term interest to replace Van Dijk, should the Dutchman leave in the summer.

Pundit Andy Reid believes Manchester United’s problems extend far beyond manager Michael Carrick, identifying three major issues that need to be addressed before the club can recover from its current crisis.

Carrick is facing mounting pressure following Manchester United’s horrid start to the 2026/27 Premier League campaign.

The Red Devils currently sit 18th in the table with just eight points from ten matches.

However, Reid believes changing the manager alone would fail to address the deeper problems affecting the club.

Speaking on BBC Radio 5 Live, Reid argued that Carrick and his players must accept responsibility for United’s performances but insisted that the problems extend into the club’s leadership structure.

He was particularly critical of the defensive mistakes that have repeatedly undermined United this season.

Comparing some of the mistakes to those that would disappoint coaches at under-15 level, Reid made it clear that the players cannot escape scrutiny.

However, he also suggested that the problems go beyond coaching decisions.

While Carrick is responsible for preparing the team and improving results, the former midfielder believes the club is facing wider challenges.

“I think the problem runs deeper than Michael Carrick. I think he’ll take his share of the responsibility for it, no doubt about it, and the players have to as well.

“And when they step out on the pitch they can’t be making the mistakes that they are, if you [saw] some of the mistakes they made tonight in an under-15s game you’d be disappointed.

“They need to sort that out. But it runs deeper. There’s football leadership, ownership and financial issues that need resolving, there’s a bigger picture at the moment.”

Reid’s assessment is backed up by Manchester United’s recent history.

Since Sir Alex Ferguson stepped down in 2013, the club has cycled through a series of managers, including David Moyes, Louis van Gaal, Jose Mourinho, Ole Gunnar Solskjaer, Erik ten Hag, Ruben Amorim and now Michael Carrick.

Despite significant investment in the playing squad over the years, the club have failed to establish the consistency that defined Ferguson’s remarkable tenure.

There have been periods of improvement and domestic trophy success, but sustained Premier League title challenges have remained elusive.

Reid’s argument suggests that appointing another manager would not necessarily resolve those long-standing difficulties.

Instead, he believes United must address the structures supporting the football operation if they are to achieve lasting improvement.

That said, Carrick’s job remains under pressure with question mark remaining on whether he will be able to survive this international break.

The dramatic 4-4 draw vs Aston Villa means United go into the break in relegation zone, just one point ahead of 20th placed Crystal Palace.

Although the comeback showed character, it does not change their league position.

If Carrick survives the break, his next challenge will be a trip to Anfield against Liverpool, who have not yet won a Premier League game at home this season.

Trump says renewed Iran strikes ‘highly unlikely’ if talks progress but warns he could still strike

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

President Donald Trump said he is "highly unlikely" to restart open war with Iran, but warned he could still strike the country as Washington tries to reach a deal with Tehran to end the conflict.

The now more than eight-month-old war, which stoked inflation and sent oil prices surging, has seen repeated bursts of fighting separated by ceasefires and peace talks for months. It was widely speculated that Trump might restart open conflict after the midterm elections, where his Republican Party lost badly to Democrats.

Trump's comments came during an interview with CNN, where he said restarting the war is unlikely as long as talks to constrain Iran's nuclear program and reopen the Strait of Hormuz make progress.

"It depends on whether or not they sign a very strong agreement," Trump said. "The answer is highly unlikely it's going to be necessary. They're a failing nation."

Trump, however, left the door open for additional strikes if he deems it necessary. The president reiterated his threats to strike bridges and power plants in Iran if the war restarts.

"I've got three aircraft carriers here. The other one just arrived. We have three. They're fully locked and loaded," the president said. "We have 38 bridges in our site that we can knock down in literally one hour."

An end to the war and reopening of the Strait of Hormuz would likely ease oil prices, though finding a truce has proven difficult. All previous attempts to end the war and reopen the strait have been met with disappointment.

Trump is likely to come under increased pressure to end the conflict, however, now that Democrats have won control of both the House and the Senate. Some Republicans are also likely to be eager for an end to the war, given the role high gas prices played in their drubbing at the ballot box.

The president has demanded Iran fully open the Strait of Hormuz and agree to never develop a nuclear weapon as part of any deal.

Later in the day on Truth Social, Trump posted that "Task Force Iron Gate is in full force and effect in the Islamic Republic of Iran."

"Coupled with history's greatest Naval Blockade, very little gets into, or out of, Iran!" he said.

Central Command announced Task Force Iron Gate on Saturday, describing it as an initiative aimed at "preventing the movement of advanced weapons systems, dual-use technologies, sanctioned materials, and illegal revenue streams across Iran's land borders and the Caspian Sea."

The U.S. has maintained a naval blockade of Iranian ports for months.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has admitted to having an affair with a woman who is allegedly a Chinese spy. His apology came in a letter to Nvidia staff, which you can read in full below. Nvidia's board of directors released a statement late on Sunday to address the bombshell report that U.S. intelligence believes Huang had an affair with a Chinese spy. The board says it has no reason to believe sensitive corporate information or U.S. national security was compromised, but the company intends to examine all available information to determine what happened.

The BoD claims it is fully confident in Jensen Huang and his leadership team. Huang, who has been married to his wife, Lori Huang, for 41 years, says he fully supports the investigation.

"A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation," a statement by Nvidia's BoD reads. "The Board prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of Nvidia and its shareholders. […] Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it."

On Friday, the first reporting emerged claiming U.S. intelligence agencies believe Huang had a romantic relationship with Alice Huang, also known as Huang Le, a former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed who was allegedly recruited by China's Ministry of State Security (MSS) to gather information and help Chinese companies obtain restricted AI GPUs. The company has been on the radar for smuggling restricted hardware to China since at least 2025: Megaspeed has been accused of smuggling $2 billion worth of AI chips into China via its subsidiary Speedmatrix.

To my colleagues at NVIDIA, I owe my family and all of you an apology.

Nvidia has strenuously rejected suggestions of improper dealings, saying that its CEO regularly meets thousands of customers and that these interactions do not involve sensitive corporate information or national security secrets. Chinese officials have also dismissed the espionage allegations as unfounded.

The suspected Chinese intelligence operation targeting Jensen has not been independently confirmed as a fact. The relationship is alleged to follow the typical "honeypot" strategy, wherein foreign intelligence agencies use operatives to ensnare targets into romantic relationships to steal information and influence their decision-making.

Huang has been an outspoken critic of those opposed to limiting the supply of AI GPUs to China. Huang recently expressed his opinion that the label 'China hawk' is not a badge of honor Americans should wear; it is really 'a badge of shame.' Huang also said it "doesn’t have to be all us or them. It could be us and them," drawing fire from others in the administration and other tech companies, including the CEO of Palantir.

Get Tom's Hardware's best news and in-depth reviews, straight to your inbox.

"I owe my family and all of you an apology," Huang wrote. "I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry. Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it. To my wife and family: I love you. I know an apology cannot undo what I have done. I hope our family will heal and that, over time, I can earn back your trust."

In June 2018, Intel chief executive Brian Krzanich resigned after an internal investigation, assisted by external legal counsel, confirmed that he had engaged in a consensual romantic relationship with an Intel employee, which violated the company's non-fraternization policy applicable to all managers. Krzanich, who had led Intel since 2013, also stepped down from the company's board, while then-chief financial officer Robert Swan took over as interim CEO. For now, it remains unclear if Jensen will meet a similar fate and lose control of the company he founded.

Jensen's full letter to staff reads as follows:To my colleagues at NVIDIA, I owe my family and all of you an apology. I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.

Throughout my life, my family has given me love and support. They deserve my honesty and loyalty. Instead, I let them down and damaged the life we built together. I take full responsibility for what I did and the hurt I caused, and I accept the criticism that comes with it.

To my wife and family: I love you. I know an apology cannot undo what I have done. I hope our family will heal and that, over time, I can earn back your trust.

To my colleagues at NVIDIA, including those I have worked alongside for decades, and to our board, partners, and shareholders around the world: you deserve better from me. Leading NVIDIA is the privilege of my life. My responsibility is to serve you and be worthy of your trust. I have fallen short of that responsibility. I am fully committed to NVIDIA and will work to earn back your confidence.

Regarding the national security concerns raised in news reports, NVIDIA’s Board of Directors has initiated an independent investigation. I support the investigation and will cooperate fully. I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America’s national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American.

My family bears no responsibility for my choices. I do. Please give them privacy.

The whole statement from Nvidia's Board of Directors reads as follows:The Nvidia Board is initiating an independent investigation in light of recent reporting that founder and CEO Jensen Huang has been the target of a foreign intelligence effort.

A committee of independent directors, assisted by outside independent counsel, will oversee the investigation.

The Board prioritizes U.S. national and economic security, which is paramount to the interests of NVIDIA and its shareholders.

While the Board has no reason to believe that either national security or NVIDIA secrets were compromised, the Board's inquiry will examine all available information.

Jensen fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it.

The Board has confidence in Jensen and his leadership team and will provide further updates as appropriate.

Birthday at Shaw’s! (Day 161)

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

It’s my 29th birthday today! A golden one at that. It’s so cool to spend my birthday on the AT at Shaw’s Hiker Hostel with my dad, Dreamcatcher and Small Bites; these amazing people I love and have experienced so much with. As you might be able to tell, I’m quite sappy about it all. We’re in the last leg of this adventure and I’m soaking it all in. Also, I noticed I have not taken a lot of pictures today. Sorry about that!

It’s also our last zero-day of the entire trail, so we’re making it count! We all relaxed and had some fun. I even got some presents! Do you remember that I lost my cool Trek hat at the Lake of the Clouds hut in The White Mountains? Well, Dreamcatcher just bought me the exact same hat again! She also gave me a little stuffed porcupine to bring along with me. They’re my favorite quirky animals on trail. These gifts are so thoughtful and awesome! I truly appreciate it.

Dutch people have an unique way of doing birthdays. Guys congratulate guys with a handshake, girls with three kisses on the cheek (left, right, left). Then everyone congratulates everyone else with my birthday too. You sit in a circle, bring out some form of charcuterie board and snacks and talk about politics. That last part might just be at our birthdays, oh well.

Dreamcatcher prepared the charcuterie board and we all emulated the Dutch birthday party for a little bit, which was fun. If she hadn’t done enough already, Dreamcatcher had another surprise, but she needed time and I wasn’t allowed in the common area for a while. Okay.. interesting. I guess I’ll play some chess with my dad! This time I did win! That’s… suspicious..

While being way too curious about the surprise, I called my family, texted back some friends, relaxed and did 9 days worth of resupply. Shaw’s offers a food drop halfway in the 100-mile wilderness. We’re doing it. This way we don’t have to rush through it, can take our time and soak it all in before we’re done.

My dad took us all out to dinner. Monson is very tiny, so we didn’t have to think for too long where to eat. He even called ahead to check if they had lobster rolls for me, which they did! I can’t call myself a proper tourist if I didn’t try a lobster roll in Maine! It was delicious, a very much appreciated little bit of luxury before heading into the wilderness tomorrow. Take that Timmy!

Back at the hostel, Dreamcatcher revealed her surprise. It’s an apple pie! One she made herself all from scratch in the hostel kitchen. It’s the first pie she ever made and she did a great job! They decorated it with candles, lighted them on and asked me to do a wish. I hope it comes true. I’m so grateful for all the effort everyone made to make this birthday truly unforgettable.

How to get the Gold Lil’ Uzi Vert skin in Fortnite

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The second week of Fortnite Treasure Hunters is all about Lil’ Uzi Vert, and you can get his Gold skin to add to our collection. However, this will be easier said than done once you explore the core method.

Let’s find out how to get this character skin added to your Treasure Hunters rewards.

The Lil’ Uzi Vert skin isn’t part of any pass if you’re wondering. Instead, you can get it for free by simply opening the chests on your runs. Chests now have a different look about them, matching with the spirits of the season.

The Lil’ Uzi Vert skin is part of the several items that are in the pool of rewards for week 2. These rewards, including the skin itself, will remain in the pool until the next Sunday, Nov. 15. While some rewards, like the Pirate Shop access token will continue in the pool of rewards, the same can’t be said about the Lil’ Uzi Vert skin.

Hence, you’ll want to open as many chests as possible. Playing in squads also help since you can open more chests together. Everyone gets the skin if one member manages to get the skin as a loot drop.

You can also target the different vaults that are located around the map. Opening them will reward you with Rare Chests that have better drop rates for rarer items.

Thanks to Epic Games, we now have a fair idea about the exact drop rate of this golden skin. Under ordinary conditions, you’ll have a 1:1562500 chance of obtaining the gold skin from a regular chest.

This makes it rarer than the Pirate Shop access token, which is remarkable to say the least. Power Hours will improve the odds in your favor, but it’s fair to say that the skin isn’t going be dropping around like eggs.

If you find it, consider yourself to be extremely lucky. Otherwise, the next Power Hours on Nov. 14 will be your best shot when the odds will be boosted by 7.5x.

Forget The McDonald's Egg McMuffin: The Spicier Option With Meaty Flavor

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

McDonald's Egg McMuffin helped put the chain's breakfast on the map, and while it remains one of the fast food joint's biggest sellers, it has since been joined by a pretty terrific supporting cast of fellow menu items. For example, the (surprisingly unseasoned) hash browns are a crispy and craveable addition to the lineup. But while the Egg McMuffin can certainly be meat-heavy (especially if you order it Swedish-style), another item brings plenty of meaty flavor to the table, along with a spicy element, too. Have you guessed it? It's the Sausage Burrito.

The Sausage Burrito features a soft tortilla, which encloses little nubs of pork sausage, pillowy scrambled eggs, melted cheese, and a mixture of tomatoes, onions, and green chiles, the latter of which are mostly mild but give the breakfast item just a kiss of spice. The burrito — which sells for $1.79 (price varies by location) — also comes with your choice of mild picante sauce or a hotter version if you like more heat.

One Reddit user said they've given "fancier" breakfast burritos a shot before, but "McDonald's with the small, dinky little burritos are still the best." It's a sentiment that is echoed around the internet, with fans declaring that while they aren't much to look at, they nonetheless fill a little burrito-sized hole in their hearts.

Not to pit two delicious McDonald's breakfast options against each other, but there are a few reasons why you might opt for a Sausage Burrito over a McMuffin. The first is that, if wrapped properly, burritos are much easier to eat on the go, including in your car, thanks to the fact that everything is contained inside the tortilla (versus the McMuffin, in which everything is layered and could slip around and possibly out of the English muffin). "Just ate two [of the burritos] on a road trip, loved 'em," one Redditor enthused. If you love spice, another user joked that "the rule is half a packet of picante per bite."

Another reason you might order the Sausage Burrito from McDonald's is if you have a taste for something with a bit more depth than your average breakfast sandwich. There's a symphony of flavors involved in the burrito, and aside from the slight spice of the filling, there are actually quite a few elements that go into these little tubes. The aforementioned green chiles and onions add a mild kick and savory depth, while the tomatoes (a known umami powerhouse) help give the burrito its fresh and surprisingly complex flavor profile.

He's Wanted: Man Utd May Have Another JJ Gabriel Situation as 17 y/o Yet to Sign Deal

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Manchester United could have another JJ Gabriel situation on their hands, with another youngster yet to sign a new contract at Old Trafford, and he's attracting plenty of interest.

GIVEMESPORT understands that Gabriel is closing in on a move to Spanish giants Real Madrid after he previously made it clear that he wanted to have his registration terminated in order to leave the club.

Gabriel has been linked with almost every big club around Europe, but it appears that Madrid are close to winning the race for his signature.

United will be entitled to some compensation when it comes to Gabriel's departure, but it's certainly going to be minimal in comparison to the sort of fee they'd want for a player of his potential.

Unfortunately for United and many clubs, they are always left in difficult situations when it comes to young talents without professional contracts, and the Red Devils could have a similar situation with another one of their youngsters.

According to a report from Telegraph journalist Mike McGrath, Manchester United youngster Jariyah Shah is the latest academy product to be attracting interest from around Europe.

The 17-year-old talent has been watched by Juventus, Monaco, Sturm Graz, and Dutch clubs, and he too could move for a small fee due to the fact that he's yet to sign a professional contract with the Red Devils.

Unlike Gabriel, Shah hasn't indicated that he's unsettled, but with a host of different clubs showing an interest in him, there's every chance he follows in the footsteps of the Real Madrid-bound teenager.

It's believed that United are expected to offer professional terms to Shah, but he's set to play at the U17 World Cup, where even more clubs will likely be keeping a close eye on him if he performs.

Man Utd are eyeing a move for a new left-back target in January after an intense scouting mission.

Shah was born in and represents Australia at international youth level, and he made the move to United a few years ago.

He joined the club at U13 level and signed scholarship terms with United back in 2025. Shah joined United from Doncaster Rovers and made his U18 league debut in February 2025.

Shah has played nine times for United's U18 side in the U18 Premier League so far this season, scoring twice and providing an assist.

Shah is eligible to represent England, Australia, Pakistan, and Vietnam, but has only represented Australia at youth level so far in his career.

Of course, losing Shah wouldn't appear to be as big a blow as Gabriel, but he's clearly a highly rated talent given the number of clubs keeping a close eye on his development.
