Celtic pyschodrama engulfing team as VAR delivers mother of all smackdowns

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Celtic built on their uplifting midweek victory over Besiktas but found the going hard against Neil McCann’s well-drilled team.

The champions possibly should have won by more than just Liam Scales’ 25th minute goal. However, Kasper Hogh’s woes are threatening to become a psychodrama afflicting the entire team.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The Dane isn’t playing badly. Never mind the goal he scored after 13 minutes that was then very harshly chopped off for a foul in the build-up. Another shot midway through the second half appeared goal-bound until a heroic block from substitute MJ Kamson-Kamara deflected the ball over the bar. Events seem to be conspiring against him.

Hogh was subbed off with 20 minutes left to very warm applause. He was sitting on the bench when he heard his name surprisingly announced as the man of the match. If this was an attempt to encourage the striker, it seemed a bit cringingly overboard.

Hogh was also on the bench when Celtic were awarded a penalty after 75 minutes so couldn’t grab the ball in an effort to put everything to bed. Nygren accepted the responsibility instead and saw Nico Mantl make a brilliant diving block to keep Kilmarnock in the game.

The fans certainly appreciated Hogh’s endeavours and clearly believe that while the statistics books might state otherwise, he has already morally broken his Parkhead duck.

His neat finish was cruelly chalked off - after the Dane had completed the understandably comprehensive celebration routine - for a foul by Mika Baur in the build-up that very few saw. Referee John Beaton nevertheless seemed very sure having been called to his monitor and spent little time deliberating.

A clearly agitated Hogh then wasted a good chance to score shortly afterwards although credit should go to Mantl, the true man of the match, for blocking with his foot when the striker had just the keeper to beat. Mantl's later penalty stop - after a VAR check confirmed a handball by Aaron Tshibola had taken place inside the box – meant bottom-of-the-table Kilmarnock were still fighting until the end.

They came so close to taking the point their efforts certainly deserved. With the clock having ticked into the four minutes of additional time, substitute Eric Ring reached the byline and swung in a cross that Ieuan Owen somehow failed to connect with in the middle.

As for Celtic, with their No. 9 seemingly so out of touch and luck, their No. 5 stepped in. Scales scored the kind of goal that is eluding Hogh at present after he got on the end of Duran’s floated cross. The ball kissed the crossbar before landing in the net. That’s three goals for the defender this season – equal with Hogh.

A planned period of silence to commemorate the war dead was cut short before kick-off due to the volume of booing in the stadium. Beaton barely gave it ten seconds before blowing his whistle. It all felt a little unsettling with Celtic taking longest to get themselves focused, at the risk of very nearly conceding the first goal. As was the case at the death, the visitors came close to scoring right at the start. Tom Lowery even had two bites at the cherry, with Celtic goalkeeper Sam Johnstone the equal to his efforts both times.

Celtic sought to take advantage of the let-off and immediately got on with the seeming job in hand: helping Hogh get off the mark at Parkhead. Nygren tried to place a cross on his head after eight minutes but the striker was crowded out. It was almost as if the team had been specifically set up to let Hogh score. And he did. 4.13pm, the moment Celtic Park had been waiting for – or so it seemed.

With Jota, making his first start since April 2025, and Camilo Duran either side of Hogh and Nygren in the space behind, it ought to have been a centre-forward’s dream. Especially given all these players seemingly had one aim in mind: feed the Hogh.

Parkhead exploded with joy unconfined. Duran slipped the ball through to Nygren, who swivelled and played in Hogh. The Dane could not miss and to be fair, he didn’t. A very composed finish into the corner had him pointing to the heavens and crossing himself and making all manner of gestures of gratitude before VAR delivered the mother of all smackdowns: Goal disallowed.

Celtic secured the three points in the end, albeit with one or two scares. They have reached the latest international break in marginally better shape than last time, when successive Rangers defeats meant Martin O’Neill’s job seemed in very real jeopardy.

Sabatini: “Il Napoli ha giocato le tre partite più spettacolari della stagione”

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Il giornalista Sandro Sabatini, attraverso un video pubblicato sul proprio canale YouTube, ha analizzato il pareggio per 1-1 tra Napoli e Lazio: “Le tre partite più belle, spettacolari, ricche di gol, emozioni e occasioni di questa stagione sono state Inter-Napoli 3-2, Napoli-Roma 3-3 e Napoli-Lazio 1-1. Il calcio dominante, propositivo e moderno si è visto in queste tre partite del Napoli, che sono state tra le migliori di un campionato che, magari dopo la sosta, potrà riservare qualche correzione. È proprio il Napoli di Allegri ad augurarsi questa correzione, perché la classifica parla chiaro. Nonostante alcune prestazioni all’altezza della situazione, gli azzurri si ritrovano staccatissimi, intorno al decimo posto, con un divario obiettivamente incolmabile per un’eventuale lotta scudetto e quasi incolmabile persino per il quarto posto. Il calcio è così, è paradossale. Non sempre si riesce a trovare una conferma o una chiave di lettura per qualsiasi partita, a meno che non ci si voglia lasciare guidare dalle ideologie e dai commenti precostituiti, anziché dalle idee e dall’analisi. Fatta questa premessa, Napoli e Lazio hanno pareggiato 1-1 al termine di una partita molto emozionante, spettacolare e combattuta. Il primo tempo è stato maggiormente favorevole alla Lazio, che infatti lo ha concluso in vantaggio per 1-0. Nella ripresa il Napoli ha trovato il pareggio, propiziato da McTominay e Hojlund, con la complicità di Pedraza, autore dell’autogol. Gli azzurri hanno poi costruito altre occasioni, neutralizzate dal portiere. Su tutte, quella clamorosa nel duello faccia a faccia con De Bruyne. Sì, il Napoli nel secondo tempo avrebbe potuto anche vincere, ma la Lazio è una squadra solida, che merita il primo posto in classifica. Gattuso l’ha organizzata benissimo. Mi è piaciuto tantissimo il primo tempo della Lazio, ma anche la gestione degli ultimi dieci minuti, nei quali ha giocato da grande squadra. Bisogna considerare che anche la Lazio, così come il Napoli, ha dovuto fare i conti con assenze importanti. A un certo punto è stata costretta a rinunciare a Sutalo e Nuno Tavares, oltre a dover fare a meno di Zaccagni. Tre assenze pesanti, eppure la Lazio esce dal Maradona tra la soddisfazione dei propri tifosi. I sostenitori della Lazio di Gattuso festeggiano, ed è giusto che sia così. Quelli del Napoli di Allegri, invece, se la prendono con tutti, a cominciare dall’allenatore, per arrivare a quello che sembra essere diventato l’imputato numero uno. Imputato di che cosa, poi, non si capisce bene. Pensate che Noa Lang, da un certo momento in avanti, è stato fischiato per tutta la partita. Eppure Noa Lang è stato nettamente il migliore in campo del Napoli. Un giocatore vivo, sempre pronto a puntare l’uomo. Romano Floriani Mussolini, alla sua prima presenza da titolare, ha vissuto una giornata difficilissima, tanto da essere sostituito da Lazzari, perché nessuno riusciva a fermare Lang. Eppure i tifosi del Napoli lo fischiavano. Per che cosa? Per un gesto? Per una sorta di regolamento di conti? Non si capisce. Credo sia stato qualcosa che non dovrebbe trovare spazio nel giudizio su Lang. Naturalmente i tifosi possono fare quello che vogliono, ma il rendimento di un calciatore di personalità, come lo è Lang, un folle ma dotato di personalità, non dipende certo dai fischi o dagli applausi. Il rendimento di un giocatore o di una squadra non dipende dagli applausi o dai fischi dello stadio. E la dimostrazione, per certi versi, arriva proprio dalla Lazio. Mi dispiace dirlo, perché la passione dei tifosi laziali, compresi quelli che non vanno allo stadio, è fuori discussione. Però la dimostrazione che il pubblico conta fino a un certo punto arriva proprio dalla stagione della Lazio, che gioca in uno stadio quasi deserto eppure è in testa al campionato. Una squadra che sembrava destinata a lottare per non retrocedere sta invece lottando per lo scudetto. Vedremo quali saranno gli altri risultati, ma una cosa è sicura: chi, dopo queste prime giornate e le prime due soste, esclude la Lazio dalla lotta scudetto, secondo me commette un errore. È una squadra cresciuta con Gattuso, così come è cresciuto lo stesso allenatore, tornato, secondo me, alla semplicità, alla razionalità, alla precisione, al lavoro e alla solidità del suo primo staff, senza tutte le confusioni dell’esperienza in Nazionale. Quindi complimenti alla Lazio, ma per una volta complimenti anche al Napoli. Nel Napoli, Milinkovic-Savic ha parato quello che doveva parare, poi è stato impallinato da Frattesi. Sul gol del primo tempo il pallone gli passa praticamente accanto alla faccia. Per il resto è stato preciso negli interventi. Beukema un po’ meglio rispetto alle precedenti esibizioni, anche se non ci voleva molto. Rrahmani si vede che sta tirando avanti, lottando nonostante una condizione fisica complicata e dolori che potrebbero anche metterlo in difficoltà. Eppure continua a combattere con grande leadership. Rafa Marín discreto. Aveva offerto qualcosa di meglio nel corso della stagione, ma la sua resta una prestazione sufficiente. Olivera corre più di tutti, soltanto che poi sbaglia più di tutti sotto porta. È un giocatore che, se andassimo a vedere gli xG di questo campionato, probabilmente scopriremmo essere andato vicino al gol diverse volte, anche con salvataggi sulla linea. Poverino, non riesce mai a trovare la soddisfazione della rete. Nel secondo tempo ha avuto un pallone da calciare con il destro, che non è il suo piede. Era quasi un rigore, leggermente decentrato, ma lo ha mandato oltre la traversa. McTominay molto bene nel secondo tempo, così come De Bruyne, soprattutto quando i due si sono invertiti di posizione. Gilmour geometrico, didascalico e niente più. Vergara è partito molto male, ma ha concluso meglio. A me piace anche senza i gol, perché è un giocatore che, quando troverà maggiore fiducia, autostima e leggerezza mentale, potrà diventare molto più incisivo. E poi c’è Lang. È un giocatore che può dividere. Può risultare simpatico o, più probabilmente, antipatico. Può essere considerato un professionista serio, anche se non credo sia questa l’immagine che trasmette, oppure un folle, uno scapestrato, un genialoide, un artista, un rapper. Può essere considerato tutto. Ma se i tifosi del Napoli fischiano Lang per aver fatto quel gesto, significa che la competenza calcistica viene superata dall’umore, dall’invidia o da qualcosa che non ha nulla a che fare con il calcio e, se posso permettermi, nemmeno con il sostegno di cui questa squadra ha bisogno. Lo dico per i tifosi del Napoli, ma anche per quelli dell’Inter, della Roma, per quelli che l’anno scorso contestavano le proprie squadre e per quelli della Fiorentina. Se si va allo stadio concentrandosi sul fischiare un giocatore e sul trovare un capro espiatorio, come è successo anche a Lucca nell’ultimo quarto d’ora, va bene, si può fare tutto. I tifosi ne hanno il diritto, ma l’appoggio è un’altra cosa. Ed è proprio l’appoggio che i tifosi dovrebbero dare. Per me Lang è un giocatore di livello internazionale. È ovvio che non giocherà tutte le partite come questa, ma le sue qualità non si discutono. Concludendo, la Lazio è in corsa per lo scudetto. Il Napoli, se dovesse continuare a giocare così, potrebbe tornare a lottare per un posto tra le prime quattro. Ma deve continuare su questa strada”.

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Jennifer Garner pulled back the curtain on a sweet chapter during her marriage to Ben Affleck when she took the pair’s three kids on a European vacation: “It was wonderful.”

Jennifer Garner is thinking back to a time when she was thriving during her marriage to Ben Affleck.

The 13 Going on 30 alum detailed the unforgettable vacation she took with her and the Gone Girl star’s kids Violet, 20, Fin, 17, and Samuel, 14, before calling it quits with Affleck in 2015.

“When my kids were really little, I went to Paris,” Garner told Ina Garten on a recent episode of her Happy Hour podcast. “Ben was shooting some Batman-ish movie in London and we went for six weeks. I took them out of school. It was the last time I really took kids out of school, but it was wonderful. It was incredible. It was so fun.”

“And part of that is that we bopped around,” Garner recalled. “We went to Venice, we went to Paris.”

And while the Batman v Superman: Dawn of Justice actor was busy getting into character as Bruce Wayne, it was Garten who suggested the Alias actress explore the French city by boat.

Since then, it’s turned into a full-blown tradition for the Daredevil actress. How fabulous is that?

Garner and Affleck, both 54, tied the knot in 2005 before separating in 2015. And while they finalized their divorce in 2018, they’ve remained a team in co-parenting their little ones.

But in all seriousness, Affleck expressed that he couldn’t be more “grateful” to have a co-parent like the Juno actress.

Crystal Palace are set to sack Pierre Sage with the club bottom of the Premier League

Kaia Gerber Shares Candid Quote About Grief After Presley Gerber's Death

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Kaia Gerber is reflecting on the grieving process after brother Presley Gerber’s death.

Kaia, 25, shared a photo of a stack of books to her Instagram on Sunday, November 8, alongside a quote from Joan Didion’s The Year of Magical Thinking.

“Grief, when it comes, is nothing we expect it to be. Grief has no distance. Grief comes in waves, paroxysms, sudden apprehensions that weaken the knees and blind the eyes and obliterate the dailiness of life,” the quote read.

The post was uploaded by Kaia and the Library Science official Instagram account, a joint book program established by the actress and Alyssa Reeder. The upload comes less than two months after news broke that Presley died at age 27.

In September, the Santa Monica Police Department told Us officers responded to a call regarding a possible overdose at the sober living facility Resolutions Living. Authorities found an unresponsive adult male at the scene. (Presley had previously been candid about his mental health and addiction struggles.)

“The Los Angeles County Department of Medical Examiner has identified the individual as Presley Gerber, 27,” authorities said in a statement. “SMPD detectives are investigating the death as a suspected overdose. At this time, there is no indication of foul play. The official cause and manner of death will be determined by the Los Angeles County Department of Medical Examiner.”

One day later, the Los Angeles County Medical Examiner confirmed that Presley’s body was “ready for release.” Presley’s cause of death was “deferred,” per records viewed by Us, and his body was later cremated.

His death has since resulted in an investigation into the sober living facility after a reform advocate with no relation to Presley reportedly filed a formal complaint. Page Six later reported in October that the California Department of Health Care Services (DHCS) determined there wasn’t enough evidence to substantiate any wrongdoing on the part of the Resolutions Living treatment facility.

Presley, the son of Cindy Crawford and Rande Gerber, was honored in a statement by his family in October.

“We are beyond heartbroken to lose our beloved son and brother. We feel so blessed to have had Presley in our lives for 27 years,” the family wrote at the time. “He is a sweet, beautiful soul who loved and is loved, now and forever.”

If you or someone you know is struggling with substance abuse, contact the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA) National Helpline at 1-800-662-HELP (4357).

Extraction shooters need a PvE mode for casual players—so what’s the controversy?

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

This idea isn't half as controversial as half of you make it out to be.

Any major extraction shooter release inevitably summons the age-old debate of PvE vs. PvP. It’s been the case with ARC Raiders and DMZ, and that schism will resurface whenever the next one launches. You can always count on heated arguments stirring controversy about something that shouldn’t be controversial at all.

After all, how dare players enjoy PvE in a PvPvE game? The way the debate often goes, you’d assume it was a cardinal sin, like the PvE crowd was planning to personally tar and feather every PvP player in existence.

Instead, PvE players often just want a more forgiving, relaxed experience that does not involve getting ratted to death in Stella Montis or having to spend an entire match in Hajin with one’s butthole clenched so tightly that not even a Wi-Fi signal passes through it. And from the looks of it, this isn’t a small crowd, either.

Take ARC Raiders, for example. The demand for a PvE mode was so large that Embark tested a toggle that disabled damage between players last month. Before then, 37 percent of its fanbase (and growing) were focused on PvE cooperation, 34 were there purely for PvP (stable since launch), and 27 landed in the middle, Embark said. Over 51 percent of all surveyed users showed interest in a dedicated PvE mode, according to the studio.

If even the thought of discussing this makes you feel like Ben Affleck with a cigarette, you’re not the only one who’s exhausted. The PvE vs. PvP argument is the reheated leftovers from the skill-based matchmaking debate, annoyingly inescapable when battle royales were all the rage.

Often, the higher-skill players don’t want to match against others of similar ability because their games get too difficult and stop being fun. And yes, this is exactly what happens to lower-skill players without SBMM.

Activision ran a famous experiment with its matchmaking in Call of Duty: Modern Warfare 3 in 2024. The company loosened the skill constraints for half of its players during a month. At the end of the test, it noted that the 10 percent of affected players at the top of the skill pyramid returned more often, but the remaining 90 percent were considerably less likely to come back. And that’s with a diluted SBMM.

“If [SBMM] were completely removed, we would expect to see the player population erode rapidly in the span of a few months, resulting in a negative outcome for all our players,” the study said.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

PvP is zero-sum by definition: for one to win, another must lose. Unsurprisingly, it doesn’t feel good to be on the losing side. Having the chance to opt out of that and into a different playstyle altogether shouldn’t cause this much uproar—especially not in a game where keeping gear plays a major role. And often, it doesn’t even relate exclusively to skill, but the type of experience one wants to have.

This time, it’s not even a matter of catering to one part of the audience in detriment of another, which would be an understandable concern if it made a modicum of sense. Embark isn’t going to make your fingers fall off every time you shoot another player. Having PvE in DMZ won’t make Activision cease content for Warzone or multiplayer, otherwise CoD Zombies wouldn’t exist.

Often, the people contemplated by PvE mode aren’t even in the game, but they’re yearning to be. The lack of full-on PvE in DMZ didn’t make me flock to multiplayer; it made me uninstall the game altogether. I only bought ARC Raiders because of the PvE test and the game’s aggression-based matchmaking. If that went away, I’d disappear with it. My friends did so the second the PvE test ended, and they’re eagerly waiting for the mode to return.

Extraction shooters being unapproachable made sense when they were niche. Now that every live-service seems to flirt with the genre, though, they should be more welcoming, especially if they want to draw a larger crowd.

As an avid frequenter of friendly lobbies in ARC Raiders, I’ve had more positive experiences with randoms than I can count, and that’s a big reason why I open the game. I’m having one hell of a time fighting Matriarchs, defibbing other raiders, and dropping duplicate blueprints. My archenemy was a cat bed, and I’m thrilled this was the case. It sucks when it’s tooth-and-nail all the time, as every big opponent of SBMM will tell you.

The difference is, unlike hero shooters or battle royales, extraction shooters already have a PvE element baked into them, no retrofitting required. Of course, you may have to reshuffle some things, like increasing the difficulty of the AI-controlled enemies to raise the threat and keep players on their toes. But it’s different from creating something entirely new, like if people wanted bots in Apex Legends or full-blooded PvP in the extraction-adjacent Helldivers 2. And it’s certainly not too much to ask.

There’s a reason extraction shooters were somewhat obscure before the gaming industry threw a borderline infinite amount of money at making the genre mainstream. Soulslikes aside, not everyone wants to get their teeth kicked in as a pastime. Sure, the cutthroat uncertainty from PvPvE shaped the identity of ARC Raiders, but there are ways to evoke that without fighting other players, as Helldivers 2 and GTFO have proved.

ARC Raiders has come a long way in the past year, in no small part because of the 37 percent of PvE players (and in bigger part because of the 63 percent of other raiders). Maybe the burgeoning PvE scene changes the vision of the game somewhat, and that’s fine. DMZ getting a fully PvE mode wouldn’t be entirely out of the realm of possibility, considering that’s basically what MWZ was. That’s the beauty and bane of live services: there’s always room for reinvention.

Destructoid's former Destiny 2 expert (RIP), Pedro now spends way too much time concocting builds in Warframe, playing Elden Ring, or practicing guitar. He's been writing professionally since 2019 and takes psychic damage every time someone forgets an Oxford comma.

Nvidia will investigate CEO Jensen Huang’s relationship with possible Chinese spy

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

Nvidia’s board of directors announced Sunday that it’s launching an independent investigation following reports that CEO Jensen Huang was targeted by a Chinese spy operation.

Nvidia also published a message that Huang sent to the company admitting that he had an affair with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based chip company Megaspeed (though he didn’t mention her by name). A memo circulating among U.S. policymakers earlier this year claimed that Alice Huang had been recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security to spy on Nvidia’s CEO.

“I owe my family and all of you an apology,” Jensen Huang wrote. “I was unfaithful to my wife. My actions hurt her and our children. There is no excuse for what I did. I am deeply sorry.”

Huang added that he’d “fallen short” of his responsibility to the company.

“To my colleagues at NVIDIA, including those I have worked alongside for decades, and to our board, partners, and shareholders around the world: you deserve better from me,” he said.

As the AI race between the United States and China heats up, chips, especially Nvidia’s chips, have been a major focus. Huang also seems to have a close relationship with President Donald Trump, who’s been a cheerleader of the AI (or “superintelligence”) industry.

In its announcement, Nvidia’s board said that it has “no reason to believe” that either national security or company secrets were compromised. It also said that Huang “fully supports the review and has committed to cooperating with it,” and that the board has “confidence in Jensen and his leadership team.”

The investigation will be conducted by independent directors working with outside legal counsel.

In response to earlier reports about the CEO’s relationship with Alice Huang, Nvidia had said that the pair’s interactions were “not unusual,” and that the company does not share classified information with customers.

Other companies involved in the AI boom have warned employees to be wary of possible spies, including those offering sex or romance.

Construction crews in California accidentally unearthed the bones of a large mammal that lived 40 to 45 million years ago

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Fossils found during construction in Oceanside, California, may belong to a giant hoofed mammal that lived 40 to 45 million years ago.

During routine excavation for a city infrastructure project in Oceanside, California, Joseph El Adli, a principal paleontologist with Helix Environmental Planning, spotted fossil bones about 12 feet (3.7 meters) below the surface. He recognized their importance and secured the area.

The bones belong to a large, hoofed mammal that lived 40 to 45 million years ago, the City of Oceanside announced on September 22, 2026.

The animal may be a titanothere, nicknamed “thunder beast,” or Amynodon, historically called a “swamp rhino.” Laboratory work should settle which.

He was monitoring the dig as the project’s paleontologist. The city places monitors on major infrastructure work as standard practice and to comply with California environmental laws.

The find includes several limb bones, ribs and numerous other bones and fragments. The largest piece appears to be a femur.

El Adli’s team worked for seven days to expose the bones from beneath several tons of hardened mudstone and sandstone.

The fossils were then wrapped in a plaster field jacket and moved to an off-site laboratory.

The exact location has not been disclosed, to protect public safety and the integrity of the site.

The bones came from Member C of the Santiago Formation, a thick sequence of sedimentary rock in northwestern San Diego County.

Paleontologists based the age estimate on the known age of that rock and the types of organisms found in it. The range falls within the Eocene Epoch, in a stretch of time called the Uintan North American Land Mammal Age.

According to the city, the animal roamed ancient floodplains in the area.

The same rock unit in the city has yielded Diegoaelurus vanvalkenburghae, a predator named in a 2022 description of Diegoaelurus. It belongs to the machaeroidines, the oldest group of sabertooth mammals.

Known from a nearly complete lower jaw with most of its teeth, it is the youngest machaeroidine on record. Its authors tentatively tie the group’s extinction to faunal change at the shift from the middle to the late Eocene, part of a wider turnover among mammal predators.

The fossil site that produced it, known as Jeff’s Discovery, also holds a tapir relative called Hesperaletes and a hyaenodontid predator, Limnocyon.

The paper was led by Shawn Zack of the University of Arizona College of Medicine in Phoenix, with Ashley Poust of the San Diego Natural History Museum.

Both candidates belong to Perissodactyla, the order of odd-toed hoofed mammals that includes today’s horses, rhinos and tapirs. Titanotheres are also known as brontotheres.

Oceanside officials describe them as animals roughly the size of a black rhino, with deep chests, a distinct shoulder hump and small horns.

In 2001, paleontologists collected brontothere remains from a sandstone layer at another construction site in Oceanside, near Rancho del Oro Road.

That animal, Parvicornus, stood about 5 to 6 feet (1.5 to 1.8 meters) at the shoulder, according to the San Diego Natural History Museum. It had only a very small horn, more of a bump, near each eye.

Brontotheres began as dog-size animals in the early Eocene and grew larger over roughly 20 million years. Those in South Dakota’s Badlands, about 38 to 34 million years ago, reached around 8 feet (2.4 meters) tall and 16 feet (4.9 meters) long.

The Badlands animals carried blunt, paired horns on their noses, sometimes more than 3.3 feet (1 meter) long. Males had larger horns than females.

These herbivores most likely ate soft leaves in Eocene forests. The group died out at the end of the Eocene, likely as the climate turned cooler and drier, the National Park Service notes.

Amynodon is the namesake of the amynodonts, which the American Museum of Natural History describes as large-bodied, hornless rhinos. Amynodon itself had a squat, hippo-like body, city officials say.

Their nickname comes from an older idea that they spent much of their time in water. Evidence for that is very limited.

Only Metamynodon, the last North American amynodont, may have been semi-aquatic. Its fossils turn up in sandstone laid down by rivers.

Amynodonts lived in Asia and North America from the middle Eocene through the early Oligocene. They have no living relatives and sit far from today’s species on the rhino family tree.

Amynodon had a large hollow in the skull in front of each eye socket. It likely anchored muscles for an enlarged upper lip.

At the laboratory, preparators will remove the plaster jackets. They will then harden and stabilize the fossils with museum-grade glues and resins.

Once the bones are fully freed from the surrounding rock, paleontologists expect to narrow down the animal’s precise identity.

The specimens are expected to join a museum collection, where they will be preserved and available to scientists and the public. The museum has not been named.

Several jacketed blocks have not been opened yet, and a skull may be inside one of them.

The information was obtained from the City of Oceanside.

Knicks' Tyler Kolek Covers Taylor Swift Song After Questioning Fandom

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

It seems New York Knicks point guard Tyler Kolek is singing a different tune about Taylor Swift’s fandom.

“He apologizes … the kid is OK,” shooting guard Jordan Clarkson wrote via Instagram Stories on Saturday, November 7, alongside footage of Kolek, 25, singing Swift’s “Blank Space” in the locker room after the Knicks bested the Cleveland Cavaliers. “He’s a Swiftie.”

Kolek made waves on a Thursday, November 5, livestream when he claimed Swift, 36, was among the team’s “fakest” celebrity fans.

“At the Knicks games, I would say it’s very performative because they just want to be seen at the game. They’re not necessarily fans,” he said, before singling out the Grammy winner by name. “Maybe Taylor Swift. I don’t know if I’ve seen her at any other games.”

A spokesperson for the Knicks later clarified Swift’s status as a fan in a statement.

“Taylor Swift has been a Knicks fan for many years, has a long history with Madison Square Garden,” the rep told Us in a statement of Swift, who even hosted her wedding to Travis Kelce at the venue this summer. “We are incredibly appreciative of her support of the organization.”

Swift, who has not publicly addressed Kolek’s now-controversial comments, notably attended two Knicks playoff games last season. Swift and Kelce, 37, made a joint appearance at the Knicks vs. Cavaliers — his hometown team — game in the Eastern Conference Finals.

“I’ve been a Cleveland sports fan for my entire life,” Kelce noted on his “New Heights” podcast afterward. “Getting Tay back to Cleveland and showing her my roots is always something I love doing. I showed her The Heights … not this time [but] previously before. We came in this time strictly just for the game, but getting back to Cleveland is always something I love doing.”

Weeks later, Swift was joined by the Haim sisters and Mariska Hargitay at Game 4 of the Knicks’ NBA Finals against the San Antonio Spurs.

“I got home and was like, ‘You gotta stop screaming, you’re screaming too much, you’re screaming instead of talking, you’re too excited,’” Swift recalled in her Songwriters Hall of Fame induction speech the next day, noting she yelled for “100 percent” of the matchup. “Then I got to witness the amazing performances I saw tonight, and I just kept screaming.”

She added at the time, “I never stopped screaming and this is what you get. I make no apologies for that. I’ve had a blast.”

Swift admittedly had no knowledge of sports until she started dating the Kansas City Chiefs tight end in 2023, and now, she’s a knowledgeable fan.

This Chilling Japanese Folk Horror Series Is One Of Hulu's Best Hidden Gems

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

If you're in the mood for a discrete dose of folk horror, Hulu recently added Adam Scott's terrifying horror "Hokum." But if you'd like to extend your trauma over, say two seasons of sheer terror, then "Gannibal" is for you. The show stars Yuya Yagira as a police officer who leaves the city to start over in a remote village. Here, he quickly discovers things aren't quite as idyllic as they seem.

"Gannibal" premiered in December 2022 but has failed to make much of an impact outside of Japan, despite arguably being one of the best horror series of the 2020s. Thus far, two seasons of "Gannibal" have been released, and had they hit Netflix in the United States, the show may well have become a big hit stateside. As it stands, this is one of Japan's best kept secrets and it's waiting to be discovered on Hulu.

Masaaki Ninomiya's "Gannibal" manga was originally serialized across thirteen volumes from October 2018 to November 2021. In October of that year, it was announced that "Gannibal" would be adapted as a TV series, with Shinzo Katayama directing from teleplays by Takamasa Oe.Thankfully, it turned out to be a worthy adaptation.

One of the best foreign horror shows streaming right now, "Gannibal" shares much in common with the South Korean horror movie with a 99% Rotten Tomatoes score "The Wailing." Both are set in remote villages and follow a police officer who becomes increasingly menaced by some terrifying ancient evil. But "Gannibal" departs from the movie with its tale of a cannibal conspiracy.

Yuya Yagira plays police officer Daigo Agawa, who after an unfortunate ordeal in Tokyo moves to the small mountain village of Kuge with his wife Yuki Agawa (Riho Yoshioka) and daughter Mashiro Agawa (Kokone Shimizu). At first, this secluded hamlet seems like the ideal refuge from the city and Agawa's fraught past. But things get off to an eerie start as Kuge's previous police officer has mysteriously vanished.

As Agawa and his family become more acquainted with the village and its people they discover the Goto family essentially run the town, and they don't seem like the most charming bunch. Meanwhile, a woman's body is found on the mountain, and while the Gotos claim she was killed by a bear, Agawa notices a human bite mark. Soon, his family's escape starts to turn into a nightmare. As such, this is not a horror TV show you can watch with the whole family. But it definitely deserves your attention regardless.

While you may never have heard of "Gannibal," the series has become a sensation in its native Japan. In April 2025, The Hollywood Reporter reported that "Gannibal" had become the most-viewed title in the country on Disney+ since its second season debuted in March of that year. The show also set new records for the streamer, solidifying its status as a bonafide mega-hit in Japan. In the United States, however, "Gannibal" mostly flew under the radar.

There are no "top critic" reviews for the series on Rotten Tomatoes, and it hasn't shown up on any "best horror series" lists since its 2022 debut. But it has managed to generate a small amount of buzz among non-Japanese viewers. One Redditor described the series as "grim folk horror" and was evidently left "completely wrecked" by it. Elsewhere on Reddit, another user described the series as "like 'Midsommar' meets 'Resident Evil 7'" and praised the "amazing actors, great cinematography, chilling and memorable soundtrack, and an absurdly dark story."

Letterboxd users are similarly impressed. "I just love it," writes one, who goes on to commend the series for its "sharp plot," "creepy characters," and "unsettling vibes," as well as the "splendid cinematography." Another also highlighted how the show is "beautifully shot and incredibly well-acted." Unfortunately, it seems unlikely there will be more to come. "Gannibal" Season 2 was released on March 19, 2025 and that wrapped up the story told in Masaaki Ninomiya's manga. Thankfully, if you are able to binge your way through both seasons of the series, there are plenty of great folk horror movies to tide you over.

Fabrizio Romano reveals Liverpool's stance on Giorgi Mamardashvili

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Giorgi Mamardashvili's Liverpool transfer was met with mixed reviews.

The goalkeeper was initially signed during the 2024-25 season for around £30 million.

However, he spent the entirety of that campaign on loan to Valencia as, at the time, the Reds still had Caoimhin Kelleher among their ranks.

In the end, the Georgian replaced Kelleher and, unfortunately, things haven't been that smooth sailing.

He played 20 games for Liverpool last term and, although his performances were fairly spotty, he was still a decent option.

Rumours about his future started to emerge after Alisson's contract was extended this summer and now, the Reds' stance on the Georgian's future has been revealed.

Fabrizio Romano confirms Liverpool are happy with Giorgi Mamardashvili

Even though fans could tell that Mamardashvili was nowhere near as good as Kelleher last term, he's still managed to stick around.

However, that could end up changing in the near future as the Reds have recently decided to spend £30 million on KRC Genk 'keeper, Lucca Brughmans.

The Belgian is only 18 years old but, he's already a regular starter for Genk and has been putting in some top-notch displays this term.

Thanks to this, fans are now wondering what plans Liverpool's hierarchy have in store for their squad this season with Fabrizio Romano now sharing an insight into what the Reds' fanbase have to look forward to in the future.

"There is a serious possibility this could be Alisson’s last season at Liverpool. Nothing is decided, but there is interest from several countries and he could explore those opportunities," he shared via his YouTube channel.

"Liverpool knew he was considering Juventus last summer. They extended his contract for a further season and he decided to stay.

"It’s still very early, and Liverpool could ask him to stay at the end of the season. The club are also really happy with Mamardashvili."

Liverpool must figure out if Giorgi Mamardashvili is their next Alisson

Alisson has been Liverpool's starting goalkeeper for as long as anyone can remember.

The Brazilian has been a rock between the sticks at Anfield and, when he hasn't been played, the weaknesses among Liverpool's ranks has been exposed.

In fact, there have actually been several games where Alisson's performances have single-handedly earned the Reds their points.

A lot of their success has come from how strong Alisson has been and, having Virgil van Dijk in front of him has obviously also helped with that.

Right now, most fans can't see how Mamardashvili could plug the gap left by Alisson but, if Liverpool can, they need to figure out a way to prove that to fans.

Otherwise, some serious questions are going to need to be asked.

Or, a lot of money will need to be spent trying to replace the Brazilian which, in reality, isn't all that feasible at the moment, especially since they just splashed £30 million on Brughmans in summer.

If Snap’s Specs are still ridiculously expensive, they’re also a lot more comfortable than they used to be

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Buy One Founder Summit Ticket, Get a Second for Free. Register now.

Founder Summit begins Nov. 4, 9:00 a.m. ET. Get your pass savings & deals before it starts. REGISTER HERE.

When I tried a prototype of Snap’s Specs at CES in Las Vegas earlier this year, the experience left something to be desired. The company’s AR glasses — which the company calls a “new kind of computer” — were heavy (they weighed around 8 ounces) and fit rather uncomfortably on my face. The device was also hot, and made my face feel tingly — a common side-effect of smart glasses where massive computing is happening under the hood.

Fast forward to late October, when I met back up with the Snap team at a Four Seasons in downtown San Francisco. By this time, the company had debuted Specs publicly during an event with CEO Evan Spiegel, and pre-orders of the company’s long-gestating headset were already underway.

What I can say is this: Specs has improved the form factor significantly since my first brush with the device. The glasses now weigh nearly half of what the previous version did, slimming down to 132 grams, or approximately 4.6 ounces. They were quite comfortable and, during the hour-ish that I demo-ed the device, it did not heat up like the previous version had.

Specs now comes in two sizes — 47mm and 52mm. The purpose here is purely to accommodate different head sizes, ensuring the glasses fit snugly on whoever’s face happens to be wearing them. They also now come equipped with spring hinges, which make the device more flexible and comfortable.

However, if the body of the device has improved greatly, its soul is still a work-in-progress. The glasses can be fun — but they also still seem to lack a dedicated purpose, and one struggles to imagine how, specifically, this expensive head gear ($2,200 a pop) would prove useful in a daily capacity. When I asked Snap staff what the device was really for — fun and games or productivity — the answer was vague. The device attempts to be a bunch of things at once and, in the process, ends up feeling a little rudderless.

I have racked my brain to come up with some daily use-cases for Specs. Some of the things I came up with were:

By contrast, here are some of the areas I’m still not totally sold on as Specs use-cases:

Specs is impressive for how the Snap team has managed to integrate a variety of user experiences into one unified device. There are a lot of impressive achievements here — specifically the spatial synching tech that allows two users to see the same thing simultaneously — that, with some further development, could be deployed towards some really interesting use cases.

The bottom line is that Snap’s device can continue to grow and transform as the company iterates. It’s an interesting product that — like a lot of its peers — doesn’t necessarily seem to have a clear purpose but, in time, could become many things.

What the device currently offers, however, may not be compelling enough to encourage people to shell out the cash that Snap is asking for.

Taylor Swift and Karlie Kloss Attend Travis Kelce’s Chiefs Game in Kansas City

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

The longtime friends reunited to watch the Chiefs take on the New York Jets at Arrowhead Stadium on Nov. 8.

Taylor Swift had some familiar company as she cheered on husband Travis Kelce and the Kansas City Chiefs on Sunday (Nov. 8).

The 36-year-old pop superstar watched the Chiefs take on the New York Jets at Arrowhead Stadium in Kansas City, joined by longtime friend Karlie Kloss. The supermodel, 34, was also among the guests at Swift and Kelce’s July 3 wedding at Madison Square Garden in New York City.

“Listen, I try to protect the privacy of my friends’ personal lives,” Kloss said during a July appearance on the Today show. “But what I will say is that they’re an amazing couple and I’m so happy for them.”

Swift and Kloss appeared in good spirits as they watched the Chiefs take the field for their ninth game of the season. The “Shake It Off” singer wore a red sweater as she cheered on Kelce alongside her parents, Andrea and Scott Swift. Sunday’s matchup also marked a career milestone for the tight end, who made his 200th NFL appearance.

Swift and Kloss first struck up a friendship in 2013, eventually becoming close enough to share the cover of Vogue in 2015. Their relationship later became the subject of speculation when they were seen together less frequently. In August 2023, Kloss attended Swift’s Eras Tour show in Los Angeles.

Sunday marked Swift’s fourth appearance at a Chiefs game this season. She previously attended the team’s Oct. 18 home game against the Los Angeles Chargers, watching from a suite alongside musician Este Haim and basketball star Caitlin Clark.

Earlier in the season, Swift attended the Chiefs’ Sept. 14 game against the Denver Broncos, where she was spotted with actor Tom Cruise. She returned to Arrowhead Stadium on Sept. 20 for the team’s matchup against the Indianapolis Colts.

Swift’s Oct. 18 appearance at Arrowhead Stadium came just one day after a surprise appearance at the 2026 Academy Museum Gala in Los Angeles, where she delivered the live debut of “The Fate of Ophelia” during a short set. The event honored filmmaker John Carpenter, Colman Domingo and Charlize Theron.

When is FA Cup second round draw? Ball numbers, time and how to watch

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

The draw for the second round of the FA Cup will take place on Monday 9 November after this weekend’s fixtures

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Seventh-tier Wimborne are one of nine non-league teams still fighting in the FA Cup ahead of the second-round draw.

The Dorset part-timers, of the Southern League Premier Division South, stunned League Two strugglers Crawley 2-0 on Sunday to reach the FA Cup second round for the first time in their history. Stockport survived a scare to beat Cray Wanderers of the Isthmian League Premier Division 3-2, while eighth-tier Stafford Rangers also led EFL opposition 2-0 before Grimsby avoid an embarrassing exit and eventually made it through on penalties.

Saturday saw a few non-league teams get the better of EFL foes as Kidderminster thrashed Doncaster 3-0, Carlisle battled past Salford City 1-0 and Barrow won 1-0 on their long trip to Gillingham, while seventh-tier Guiseley also upset National League Sutton United 1-0. Friday night’s matches saw 2021 champions, and former Premier League winners, Leicester City avoid a potential banana skin at sixth-tier Oxford City.

Another seventh-tier side, in Plymouth Parkway or Berkhamsted, will advance to the second round and those teams meet tonight to conclude the first round, following the second round draw on TNT Sports.

Here’s everything you need to know about the second-round draw

The draw for the second round of the 2026-27 FA Cup takes place on Monday 9 November, before the clash between non-league clubs Plymouth Parkway and Berkhamsted.

The television coverage of the game starts at 6:30pm GMT, with the draw itself expected to begin shortly afterwards.

The draw will be available to watch live in the UK on TNT Sports 1, HBO Max, and on the TNT Sports YouTube channel. Coverage begins at 6.30pm, with the draw itself expected to start at approximately 6.45pm, before kick off at 7.30pm.

There will be 40 teams in the draw for the second round with 20 fixtures to be made ahead of the entry of the Premier League and Championship clubs from the third round onwards.

The 20 FA Cup second-round matches will be held across the weekend of 5-6 of December, with the third-round draw presumed to take place on the evening of Monday 7 December.

Epic Games Launcher is coming to SteamOS

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Epic Games confirmed that its Linux launcher will officially support SteamOS and Steam Decks.

Epic Games confirmed that its Linux launcher will officially support SteamOS and Steam Decks.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

In October, Epic Games said that it was “investigating” making Fortnite compatible with Valve’s Linux-based SteamOS. At the time, Epic Games Launcher was in preview, but SteamOS was not specifically listed as a supported distro. Now, Communications Director Jake Jones has confirmed SteamOS compatibility, along with a handful of other distros, to GamingOnLinux. In response to a question about the AppImage being replaced with Debian / Ubuntu .deb downloads, Jones said:

“The new Epic Games Launcher is still in Preview and we plan to support as many Linux distributions as possible, including Fedora, SteamOS, Cachy, Omarchy, and more. We’re testing and evaluating packaging formats for distribution, and we’ll restore AppImage this month.”

That doesn’t necessarily mean Fortnite will come to SteamOS. Easy Anti-Cheat (EAC) Linux v2 support still remains an open question. When it announced its work to bring Fortnite to Linux, the company said that the Steam Deck “does not support the required features for our EAC v2 solution, but we are actively talking with Valve and hope to support it in the future.”

“Everyone needs to look at themselves” – Patrick Dorgu questions Man United teammates after dramatic Aston Villa draw

Football journalist and broadcaster who has reported on Liverpool FC since 2009, providing in-depth coverage, analysis... read more

Stan Collymore is a football pundit and former player, signing for Liverpool FC in 1995 for an English transfer record... read more

Manchester United defender Patrick Dorgu has questioned whether his teammates are doing enough to turn the club’s season around following Sunday’s dramatic 4-4 Premier League draw against Aston Villa.

The Danish international delivered an outstanding performance off the bench, registering a goal and an assist to rescue a point for Michael Carrick’s side at Old Trafford.

However, Dorgu was in no mood to celebrate his individual contribution, instead expressing frustration at United’s alarming league position.

Speaking to Sky Sports after the match, Dorgu called on every player to take responsibility for the team’s struggles and show greater commitment to Carrick and the club.

Despite helping United recover from a two-goal deficit in stoppage time, Dorgu admitted he was deeply unhappy with the result.

The 22-year-old insisted that rescuing a draw was not enough given the club’s current circumstances.

Although the team showed considerable character to recover from 4-2 down, the defender made it clear that the players cannot be satisfied with their current performances.

“I don’t feel happy, we are in a bad situation. We need to fight together to get out of it. Draw better than a loss but we had the better opportunities.

“Everyone needs to look at themselves and ask if they are doing enough. We need to fight for the manager, for the club and get out of this together.

“Of course it hurts [to be in relegation zone], there’s a lot of games and we need everyone to come back sharp from international break and start getting wins.”

Dorgu delivered a stunning individual cameo after coming on as a 66th-minute substitute to replace Diogo Dalot.

With United trailing 4-2, Dorgu single-handedly dragged the hosts back into the contest. In the 91st minute, his precise cross set up Joshua Zirkzee to reduce the deficit.

Five minutes later, Dorgu rose highest to power home a 96th-minute header from Bruno Fernandes’ corner, capping off a remarkable double comeback after United had fallen two goals behind twice.

Despite rescuing a point in stoppage time, the result leaves Manchester United sitting in the relegation zone heading into the November international break.

Sitting 18th in the table with just eight points from ten matches, United face their most alarming domestic slump in 40 years.

Carrick and his staff now face a crucial two-week pause to regroup before returning to action for a high-stakes trip to face rivals Liverpool at Anfield.

Leeds 'appalled' by alleged racist abuse aimed at Jayden Bogle after Mathys Tel tackle

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Jayden Bogle avoided a red card following a VAR review of his challenge on Mathys Tel in the first half of Saturday’s game

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Leeds United have condemned alleged online racist abuse aimed at defender Jayden Bogle after Saturday’s 1-0 Premier League win against Tottenham Hotspur at Elland Road.

Bogle avoided any booking despite planting his studs onto the Achilles of Spurs attacker Mathys Tel during a first-half tackle.

On-field referee John Brooks did not penalise the Leeds wing-back, and following a lengthy VAR review, officials determined there was no serious foul play.

In a statement released on Sunday, Leeds said: "Leeds United Football Club are appalled at the disgusting, racist social media abuse Jayden Bogle suffered following Saturday’s Premier League victory against Tottenham Hotspur.

"Leeds United condemns this behaviour in the strongest possible terms. There is no place for racism in football or society.

"The club will work with the Premier League and relevant authorities to identify the individuals and push for the strongest possible action to be taken against them.

"Jayden has the full and unconditional support of the club and his team-mates, and we all stand with him at this time. Nobody should ever be subjected to racist abuse."

Tottenham said they were "disgusted" to hear of the abuse.

"We have offered Leeds United our support in identifying those responsible and we will pursue every available avenue to ensure they are identified and face the full consequences of their actions," Spurs said in a statement.

The Premier League added on X: "Anybody found guilty of committing discriminatory abuse will face club bans and criminal prosecution."

Tel received prolonged medical attention following Bogle’s challenge and was subsequently replaced by Dominic Solanke. It is understood the north London club will write to Pro Ref to request an explanation as to why Bogle was not shown a red card.

During his post-match press conference, Tottenham boss Roberto De Zerbi avoided directly addressing whether Bogle deserved a red card.

However, the Italian voiced frustration over Brooks’ officiating during the match and confirmed the club are awaiting updates on the severity of Tel’s injury.

After Tel was forced off, Leeds went on to win the game thanks to a late goal from Noah Okafor as Tottenham’s three-match winning run came to an end.

Air Algerie Business Class Airbus A330neo Review: Utterly Awful

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

For the first long haul segment of my Orient Express adventure via Africa trip, I flew Air Algerie’s Airbus A330-900neo business class on the 6hr50min flight from Montreal (YUL) to Algiers (ALG). I had heard almost entirely negative things about Air Algerie, which intrigued me, naturally! Well, now that I’ve flown Air Algerie I can confirm that everything I heard was true, and then some.

I don’t want to be dramatic — obviously flying business class is better than economy — but honestly, just about everything sucked. On the plus side, the seats as such were fine, but that’s where the good news ends. The food was not good, and took an insanely long amount of time to be served. There were no amenity kits… or menus… or meal choice… or alcohol… or diet soda. If the crew were any more indifferent, they would’ve been invisible.

I wouldn’t fly Air Algerie again unless I absolutely had to, which… I can’t imagine that’ll ever be the case. Honestly, Air Algerie made me yearn for my recent flight on Turkmenistan Airlines, which was at least fun.

Air Algerie is a pretty isolated airline, which perhaps reflects Algeria overall — the country just isn’t interested in visitors, and getting a visa requires jumping through hoops.

So Air Algerie doesn’t have any useful partnerships when it comes to earning or redeeming miles. As a result, booking a cash ticket is just about the only option, unless you’re a frequent flyer with the airline. Air Algerie tends to have rather steep business class fares, despite its lackluster product.

However, I did manage to find a rather attractive one-way business class fare. I booked the following in business class for $1,171 in cash:

10/03 AH2701 Montreal to Algiers departing 4:10PM arriving 5:00AM (+1 day)10/04 AH4000 Algiers to Tunis departing 7:10AM arriving 8:30AM

The first segment was operated by an Airbus A330-900neo, while the second segment was operated by an Airbus A320 that was wet leased from Avion Express.

I had flown into Montreal the same day from Miami (MIA) on Air Canada Rouge, so I had an extended layover. I was at the Air Algerie check-in counter before it opened at 12:30PM, and there was already quite the queue of passengers waiting. Unsurprisingly, and as is very common for North Africa flights, people had a ton of checked bags.

It took me around 30 minutes to get checked in, since there was one counter for business class, and there were half a dozen people in front of me. At first I was asked if I had a visa for Algeria, though I explained I was in transit, which seemed to catch the check-in agent off guard. Air Algerie is clearly targeted at the local community, and doesn’t see much international connecting traffic.

Security was a breeze, and Air Algerie sends its business class passengers to the National Bank Lounge, which is the contract lounge in the international portion of the terminal. I won’t do a full review, but it was actually a pretty pleasant facility, with plenty of seating, lots of natural light, and it wasn’t very crowded.

The food selection was pretty average, with a handful of hot options, plus cheese, cold cuts, etc. There was coffee, tea, wine, beer, and soft drinks. I made the mistake of saving my hunger and thirst for onboard the Air Algerie flight!

My Air Algerie flight was departing from gate A58, with boarding scheduled for 3:10PM, a full hour before departure. I was at the gate early enough to see the inbound aircraft arrive, and goodness, what a stunner it was!

The Montreal route is primarily served by an A330neo with the registration code 7T-VLB, which is in a special livery celebrating the country’s football team. Looking at the plane was honestly the best part of the flight experience, unfortunately.

The boarding area got very full before departure, with lots of passengers in wheelchairs, and lots of crowding around the gate. Boarding ended up starting at 3:20PM, with those needing extra time, as well as business class passengers, being invited to board first.

I can’t describe how excited I was to step onboard, as I always love trying new airlines! I wasn’t fully prepared for the level of indifference shown by every part of the experience, though.

Before I get into this review, I should mention that Air Algerie has recently started taking delivery of Airbus A330-900neos as part of its fleet modernization initiative. However, the airline isn’t just taking delivery of these planes factory fresh, but has also acquired a couple secondhand from Air Belgium, which went out of business in 2024.

Air Algerie maintained the Air Belgium interiors — they also have staggered seats, but it’s a slightly different configuration. I boarded through the second door on the left, and then turned left into the business class cabin.

Business class takes up the entire space between the first and second set of doors, and consists of 30 seats, spread across eight rows, in a 1-2-1 configuration. These seats are from the Stelia Aerospace Solstys line, so they’re typical last generation staggered business class seats. When it comes to the seat details, they have 44″ of pitch, 21″ of width, and convert into 77″ flat beds.

On the plus side, it’s really easy to photograph Air Algerie cabins, since crews are nowhere to be seen. 😉

If you’re traveling with someone, you may appreciate the sets of center seats in odd numbered rows, as these are “honeymoon” seats, which allow you to sit really close to your companion. Meanwhile in even numbered rows, the seats in the center section are further from one another and really close to the aisles, so those would be my least preferred seats in this configuration.

Along the windows, seats alternate between being closer to the aisle and closer to the windows. In odd numbered rows, seats are closer to the aisle. What I particularly don’t like about these staggered seats is just how exposed some of them are to the aisle, as you really have no separation.

In even numbered rows, seats are closer to the windows, and these are the best seats in the house, in terms of offering some separation from the aisle, and an easy view out the window.

I assigned myself seat 6A, the window seat on the left side in the third to last row.

These staggered seats have a console to the side of the seat, and on the side of that is a panel with seat controls, an entertainment controller, and a small armrest.

There’s an exposed storage area above the panel, where you can store things, or even hang the headphones. As you can see, the seat just wasn’t well maintained, and also wasn’t clean, as it also needed a good wiping down.

In terms of charging, the AC power outlet and USB-A outlet were located underneath the seat. There was no USB-C or wireless charging, unsurprisingly.

Looking at the front of the seat, the tray table is stored behind the seat in front, and can be extended by being lowered and then moved over. One of the nice things about these tray tables is that you can angle them away from you, so it’s possible to get up during the meal service.

The entertainment system is in a fixed position and is located above the ottoman. The monitor here was 16″ and was touchscreen, but wasn’t terribly high definition.

As much as there are many aspects of this seat I don’t love, I do like how big the footwell is, which is great for those of us with big feet. It’s not necessarily that comfortable when you’re laying sideways in terms of space for knees, but still, there was a great amount of space here.

One other interesting note is that there were no overhead bins in the center of the cabin, so the overhead bins along the windows got pretty full, since the A330neo doesn’t have huge bins. On top of that, there were no individual air nozzles above seats, sadly. That was a problem on this flight, given how warm the cabin was kept.

All-in-all, Air Algerie’s business class hard product is okay. The cabin maintenance could definitely use some help, but honestly, this is the least of Air Algerie’s problems.

The Air Algerie business class entertainment system on the A330neo is pretty awful. For one, there’s not even a map feature. I’m sorry, I’m not sure the last time I flew an airline with seat back entertainment that didn’t have a map feature. What’s going on there?

The system did have some movies, TV shows, and music, but it was a really lackluster selection.

In total, there were 100 movies and 36 TV shows, but it definitely felt like the number of “popular” options was limited.

The airline presumably went with the cheapest entertainment package available. For example, when it comes to sitcoms, there was one episode of “Fresh Off The Boat,” and it was the pilot episode from 2015. Yikes.

When it comes to Wi-Fi, that’s something that just isn’t offered by Air Algerie. Funny enough, these ex-Air Belgium A330neos have Wi-Fi installed, but the airline just chooses not to use the system. So expect that you’re going to disconnect when flying with Air Algerie.

When it comes to amenities in Air Algerie business class, they were also very limited. The highlight was the pillow and blanket, which were at the seat upon boarding, but not exactly very nicely presented.

Anyway, this was the highlight of the amenities, and while they were nothing amazing, they were at least decent.

Headphones were also passed out well after takeoff, though the quality of them was very poor. This was closer to what you’d expect in economy than business class.

There were no amenity kits, or anything else to speak of. I don’t remember the last overnight business class flight I’ve taken where I wasn’t offered an amenity kit?

Despite the very drawn out boarding process, the crew was nowhere to be seen for most of boarding. Finally at 4:10PM, right at departure time, they came around with pre-departure drinks. There was no “hello” or “welcome onboard,” but instead, just the tray being held out with orange juice or water. At least it was served in glassware… yay?

Boarding this Air Algerie A330neo took an incredibly long time. Every seat was taken (business class seemed to be occupied mostly by operational upgrades plus friends and family). There was just endless confusion, and the crew’s only involvement with things was to occasionally check on people they knew and have chats with them, while everyone else was ignored.

Boarding was also rough because it seemed like the APU was broken, so the cabin was incredibly hot. During boarding, I had asked the flight attendant if I could use the lavatory (I try to do that as a courtesy, even though it showed as unoccupied), and she responded “wait.” I checked with her again some time later, and she again said “wait.” I didn’t end up getting to go before departure… oh well!

Our departure time came and went, and about 20 minutes later I asked a flight attendant if we were delayed. She didn’t understand my question, but after rephrasing, her answer was “no.” The door ended up closing at 4:35PM, 25 minutes behind the scheduled departure time.

At that point the manual safety demonstration began, which was exclusively performed in French and Arabic, and not in English. In fairness, I was probably the only person onboard who didn’t have French or Arabic as my first language. I should also mention there were no safety checks during taxi — some people had their seats reclined, plenty of people still had their (glass) pre-departure beverages, and the guy across from me didn’t even have his seatbelt on.

We began our pushback at 4:40PM, and then at 4:50PM we began our taxi.

While taxiing (and turning a corner), the captain made his welcome onboard announcement at 4:55PM, telling us “this is captain, good morning, taking off shortly.” Okay, we’ll ignore the “morning” part (I’ll assume it just means he’s well rested!), but I assume at Air Algerie the captain is also the one taxiing, right? If so, that’s a lot of multitasking, especially when you’re actively turning.

But hey, credit where credit’s due, this guy was good at turning corners. When we got to runway 24R at 5PM and were cleared for takeoff, we didn’t slowly make the turn and then accelerate. Instead, we started accelerating pretty aggressively in our turn, and were going over 15 knots, while turning at a 90 degree angle. Seriously, check the Flightradar24 data.

We had a very powerful takeoff roll and the captain pulled up on the sidestick as if we were in a fighter jet. The views on the climb out were gorgeous — what a lovely afternoon!

We made a sharp turn after takeoff, and had some nice views of the airport thanks to that.

The seatbelt sign was turned off around five minutes after takeoff. A little while later, we started to get a beautiful sunset view as well.

Dinner was served after takeoff in Air Algerie business class, but my gosh, what a bad and drawn out meal it was. I can deal with bad food or slow service, but bad food, bad drinks, and slow service? BLEH. The first thing to understand is that there were no menus or choices. You’re just served stuff, and it’s a “take it or leave it” situation.

Service began 50 minutes after takeoff, when warm towels were distributed.

Then around 15 minutes later, tablecloths were handed out. These felt more like large, flimsy napkins, than actual tablecloths, but whatever, no biggie.

Another 10 minutes later, the first course was served. The actual food was a little smoked salmon, some egg, some olives, etc. It didn’t taste good at all, but what was worse was the presentation. It’s as if they just threw a bunch of packaging on the tray, and put zero effort into how it’s presented.

I couldn’t help but do their job for them, and make it look a little nicer, because it’s just embarrassing. Oh, and bread is just served by hand out of the basket. Nice.

I kind of loved the Air Algerie branding on the cutlery, but that’s the same cutlery you have to use for the entire meal. For that matter, the cutlery was downright sticky when I removed it from the packaging, so clearly it just hadn’t been cleaned properly.

Drinks were brought out separately from the meal, but they might as well not have bothered. I didn’t realize that Air Algerie is a “dry” airline — it hadn’t even made my list of airlines that are dry. As it turns out, this is only a fairly recent development, as the airline switched to being dry a few years ago.

It’s not only that they don’t have alcohol, but they don’t even have any diet sodas. They have juice or full calorie soda. There was also a huge language barrier here:

“Drink?”“Do you have wine?”“Soda, juice.”“Do you have Diet Coke?”“Coke?”“No, Diet Coke.”“What?”“Coke Zero? Diet Pepsi? Pepsi Max?”*stares blankly*“I’ll have a water, please.”*serves me Sprite*

Another 15 minutes later the main course was served. Again, there was no option, and it seemed to be some sort of a swordfish, or something. As someone who typically loves fish (and orders it way too often on planes), I found this to be way overcooked, dry, and completely tasteless. If you’re not going to offer people a choice of meals, what a random thing to serve!

After an additional pause, the crew came around with cheese plates. I mean, they certainly serve quite a quantity of food, the issue is just that there are no choices, and none of it is actually good. They also don’t actually clear any plates. They just keep bringing more and more food, and if your tray is overflowing, so be it (for the below picture, I had placed the other stuff from the tray over on the side console).

Next up, two whole apples were served, which… I mean, I can’t blame them for the excessive amount of food, serving cheese, fruit, and dessert. But still, this very much feels like an afterthought. I also couldn’t help but wonder if those apples had been washed, given that we’re talking about an airline that serves bread by hand.

Lastly there was a little dessert tart trio. I tried to order a tea without caffeine, but that was a challenge:

“Coffee or tea?”“Do you have any tea without caffeine?”“Coffee or tea?”“Tea please, but no caffeine.”“Coffee?”“Tea, please.”

The entire meal service took close to three hours. Nothing about it was good. The food wasn’t good. The service was terrible. I mean, everything was just so poorly timed. There was never even the offer for more drinks (even just bottled water), and when I did ask, it took them 15 minutes and multiple reminders to actually get some more water.

I just don’t get it. As another example, they served the cheese plates, and then 10 minutes later they came around with more bread, after just about everyone had finished their cheese course. I’m exhausted writing about this. I need a drink.

The Air Algerie A330neo business class lavatory is located at the very front of the cabin. One lavatory really isn’t enough for a full business class cabin plus the crew, so there was often a wait. While there are two lavatories behind the cabin, they’re shared with economy passengers, so there’s usually a line.

The lavatories were fine. Toward the end of the flight they definitely weren’t clean, but to be honest, I’ve seen much worse lavatories on airlines in the region, so I’m hardly going to complain.

By the time the meal service was complete, we had well under four hours remaining to Algiers. So I reclined my seat into bed mode, and tried to get a bit of rest. The bed was okay, and again, compared to the rest of the experience, it was one of the better things.

The issue is how hard it was to actually get rest on this flight. The lights were fully on until after the meal service was complete, so at that point it was under four hours until our arrival in Algiers.

Then the cabin lights were once again fully turned on exactly two hours before arrival. So for those keeping track at home, on a 6hr50min flight, the lights were dimmed for around 1hr50min. I’m not sure I actually slept for a minute. The cabin was really warm, and with the lack of individual air nozzles, I just couldn’t get comfortable.

It absolutely boggles my mind why the crew has to start the pre-landing service a full two hours before landing, when the extent of the breakfast is a single tray with a small side of fruit, a small piece of cake, and a croissant. Seriously, this is what everyone is being woken up two hours before landing for?!

Credit where credit’s due, the drip coffee with milk was drinkable, and perhaps the culinary highlight of the flight. Getting a refill was beyond my abilities, but I sure did savor that one cup!

I understand that North African countries are often known for warm hospitality in a non-transactional setting, where there’s a genuine warmth and generosity to hospitality. Clearly that doesn’t apply in a transactional setting, though, and certainly not on Air Algerie.

I don’t want to say the crew was rude — that wouldn’t be fair, and I didn’t even communicate with them enough to draw that conclusion. They actually seemed to be very nice in how they chatted with their friends who were in the cabin.

As a customer, though, everything about the service was terrible. The crew had no warmth, no attentiveness, they were incredibly disorganized and slow, and the service flow just made no sense. If I were to train a crew on how to provide good service in business class, I’d have them experience this flight, and then have them do exactly the opposite. I think I’ve ragged on them enough.

You’ve gotta love how the refreshing towel has Air Algerie’s logo, and then says “Always caring for you.” If this is what care looks like, I’d love to see what not being cared for looks like!

I was excited when my Air Algerie flight was slowly coming to an end. At around 4:20AM Algiers time the seatbelt sign was turned on, and the crew prepared the cabin for landing. Now, that didn’t actually involve doing any security checks, but they made sure passengers were aware there would be no more service.

We ended up touching down at 4:50AM, and it must’ve been one of the smoothest touchdowns of my life, as you could barely feel it. From there, we had a short five minute taxi to a remote stand, where we pulled in at 4:55AM.

It’s very odd how an airport that has so many empty gates and that’s rather underutilized sends its “flagship” aircraft to remote stands, but whatever.

Upon deplaning, there wasn’t so much as a “goodbye” from the crew. There also wasn’t any priority deplaning for business class, or a separate business class bus. It was quite a long drive to the terminal, and we got there at 5:15AM, where my interesting transit experience started…

I highly recommend flying Air Algerie business class… if you want to be reminded how every other airline doesn’t actually suck that much, by comparison. Air Algerie’s A330neo business class hard product is okay, and is the least of the issues.

The problem is everything else, from the completely indifferent crew, to the unnecessarily drawn out service, to the lack of meal options, amenity kits, menus, diet soda, alcohol, etc., to the subpar entertainment and lack of Wi-Fi… the list goes on and on. And that says nothing of the bizarre elements of the flight, like no English safety demos, no safety checks before takeoff or landing, etc.

What’s sad is that I would’ve actually had a more favorable impression of the flight if they just said “this is a sleeper flight, and we have no inflight service.” Truly, no service would’ve been better than this. I’m happy I had the chance to fly Air Algerie, but you know, I think I’m good for the time being!

What do you make of Air Algerie’s business class?
