Burnley must sanction emotional Newcastle switch to fix key problem area

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Burnley have endured a nightmare beginning to the 2026/27 Championship campaign following relegation from the Premier League, as the January transfer window looks increasingly important for the Clarets to get right in each passing week.

Burnley were relegated with a whimper from the Premier League last season, but heading back into the Championship, Clarets fans would have naturally been optimistic about their chances, having been automatically promoted the three previous times they found themselves in the division.

However, finding a replacement for Scott Parker proved to be difficult, and in truth, drawn out for way too long, as Belgian head coach Nicky Hayen was handed a poison chalice upon his arrival at Turf Moor.

Hayen won just one of his 12 league games in charge in Lancashire, as well as being knocked out of the League Cup at the hands of League One Bradford City, as incoming Swiss boss Fabio Celestini will be hoping to be given until January to make some much-needed changes to Burnley ranks.

Following his swift appointment, Celestini will have had time to work with his squad in training and identify key areas of improvement, which, in the Clarets’ case right now, is all over the pitch.

However, some positions are more problematic than others, as the options between the sticks right now at Turf Moor aren’t wholly convincing.

Max Weiss started the campaign between the sticks, but after a few high-profile errors, the German was dropped in favour of French goalkeeper Gregoire Coudert, who joined from Brest in the summer.

Since replacing Weiss between the sticks, Coudert has not covered himself in much glory either, shipping four away to Lincoln City and being at fault for Queen’s Park Rangers’ equaliser in their 1-1 draw at Loftus Road, as a highly recognisable name ought to be a priority this January to fix this evident issue.

Football League World has asked AI to rank the top 25 most demanding fanbases in British football right now

If the tide doesn’t turn at Burnley sooner rather than later, the Clarets are at risk of following in the footsteps of the likes of Sunderland, Luton Town, and Leicester City to suffer double relegations from the Premier League to League One in consecutive years.

A solid pair of hands between the sticks can be a game-changer, as Celestini must look to bring out-of-favour Newcastle United shot-stopper Nick Pope back to the club in January.

Pope, 34, worked his way up the Football League with the likes of Bury Town, Harrow Borough, Welling United, Charlton Athletic, Cambridge United, Aldershot Town, York City, and Bury before earning a move to Burnley in the summer of 2016, initially acting as deputy to Tom Heaton under Sean Dyche.

155 appearances across all competitions later, Pope had announced himself as one of the finest shot-stoppers in the country, earning ten caps for England during Jordan Pickford’s dominant era, and eventually joining Newcastle United in the summer of 2022 for around £10 million following Burnley’s relegation.

At 34, Pope found himself battling with Southampton outcast Aaron Ramsdale for the number one spot at St James’ Park last season under Eddie Howe, and now is firmly behind Lukáš Horníček in the pecking order.

With Ewen Jaouen and Mark Gillespie also vying for minutes under Mathias Jaissle, Pope’s Newcastle career appears to be coming to a quiet end, as the former England international ought to push for a return to Turf Moor this winter.

Despite his age, few can deny his outstanding shot-stopping ability, and right now, you’d surely do well to find a Burnley supporter who doesn’t believe Pope would walk straight into their side right now in the Championship.

Burnley need a hero, and someone to increase the mood around the place, and given Pope’s situation at Newcastle, he could be the perfect fit.

The only obvious stumbling block is likely his wages, which may be too steep for the Clarets to match, though they would be remiss not to try, as Celestini has one obvious goal heading into January.

Do you think Burnley should look to re-sign Nick Pope from Newcastle in January, or should they be looking elsewhere? Share your thoughts below!

Burnley are in desperate need of an uptick in form sooner rather than later; otherwise, they risk being cut adrift from the rest of the pack.

The January window will provide Celestini with the chance to make some much-needed changes to his squad, and it would be a surprise if a new goalkeeper wasn’t high on his list.

The upcoming window could be Burnley’s last roll of the dice to secure survival, as the Clarets cannot afford to let it pass them by.

“We should have reacted better” | Frank admission won’t bring the points back | West Ham News

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Down in ninth place, outside even the play-off places, is hardly where West Ham, the pre-season favourites and the ‘big hitters descended into the Championship’ expected to find themselves after fifteen games. This, the most competitive of leagues, throws up unexpected results each and every week and West Ham have, simply, not been up to speed.

The week after week excuses of Nuno Espírito Santo have been replaced by the emotional, heart-on-his-sleeve reassurances of an albeit shell- shocked Scott Parker, but it is clear there’s a huge challenge ahead.

Scott Parker may have a more engaging style than Nuno but excuses only count for so long

Talk is cheap, there are only so many times we as fans we can be told that the team should have tried harder, prepared better, worked more, et cetera, et cetera, et cetera..before our eyes glaze over and disappointment gives way to anger – as was displayed at full time yesterday.

Defender Dinos Mavropanos, to his credit, takes his fair share of the blame himself for the defensive lapse that led to Blackburn’s second goal when he and his team mates switched off, anticipating a stoppage for Max Kilman’s head injury instead of playing to the whistle:

“I mean, yes, it’s tough, this league with no VAR coming around, it’s difficult to speak about these situations,” he explained

“I’m not going to blame the referee because at the end it’s a decision I can’t change. But again, I think we should have reacted better and not concede this kind of goal.” (whufc.com)

Exactly so, Dinos. However frank acceptances like this don’t bring the points back. Forget automatic promotion. Eleven points dropped in the last four games is relegation form, whichever way you look at it.

West Ham pay for the lack of competition for centre back places, as we’ve already pointed out repeatedly. Taking the blame for mistakes is one thing. Not repeating them is a whole other set of problems.

Two Neighboring $9.9 Million Oceanfront Homes Just Listed in Kauai’s South Shore—And They Can Form an Ultra-Luxe Compound

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Tucked into a private-club community in Hawaii called Kukui‘ula, two luxury homes just hit the market on Kauai's South Shore.

Set right on the ocean, and set among lush tropical vegetation, this walkable community is only 25-minute drive from Lihue Airport.

"The close proximity of everything is very convenient. You’re not having to spend a lot of time in the car,” says Pancho Sullivan of Hawaii Life, who is co-listing the two homes with Haunani Sullivan as well as Suzanne Harding and Jeff Skinner of Kukui‘ula Realty Group. The asking price sits at $9,900,000 each.

Both were built in 2025 and have never been occupied. Aepo Nei 18 and Aepo Nei 19 are neighboring 3,372-square-foot homes with four bedrooms and 4.5 baths each. They can be purchased together, to create a private compound, or by separate buyers.

Even better, the homes are fully furnished and stocked with items like bed and bath linens, beach towels, and everything you need to cook and serve in the kitchen, such as bowls, plates and glasses.

But it’s the design by Brazil-based Architects+CO that’s truly unique. Kauai Bahia Construction and their in-house material company, Kayu, handled all of the finish material production and millwork installation.

"Brazil is known for incredible resort-club communities," says Pancho. "We actually traveled to Brazil to look at some of the clubs they designed. It’s the same tropical feel (as Hawaii) that blends in really well with nature."

Aepo Nei 18’s layout features connected pavilions while Aepo Nei 19’s living spaces open onto a lawn courtyard. Design features at both include deep roof overhangs, walls of glass and multiple outdoor living spaces such as an infinity-edge pool; jacuzzi; swim-in, swim-out sauna; and in-water fire pit.

"They both have beautiful 180-degree ocean views looking across the Tom Weiskopf-designed golf course. The swim-in, swim-out dry sauna facing the ocean is a unique feature," says Pancho, adding that the usage of natural materials like lava stone, coral and teak "fit into the landscape very beautifully."

Referred to as the Garden Island, Kauai is home to about 73,000 people.

"We get the majority of the island’s sunshine down here," says Haunani, about the South Shore. "People flock here for the beaches and the sunshine, Hawaiian monk seals on the sand, and a lot of ocean activity including surfing.

"A lot of people that venture to Kauai are adventure seekers. We have great hiking. You can get on a boat and dive on the Nā Pali Coast and do ziplining and ATV tours."

Stand-up paddleboarding, diving and fishing are two other activities easy to do in this community.

Homeowners become club members with access to dining, a pool, spa (boasting a steam rook and lava-rock dry sauna), pickleball and tennis courts, and an 18-hole golf course.

"There’s an incredible gym with classes daily, from yoga to sound bathing to fitness training," says Pancho. "All of that is part of your club membership. It’s a great place to be in tune with nature and get healthy."

A 10-acre organic garden features a farm stand as well as opportunities to create floral bouquets. There’s also a conscious effort to honor Hawaiian history through cultural-heritage walks; sunrise ceremonies; hula performances; and classes in Hawaiian featherwork, quilting and basket weaving.

“Members who are part of the club become life-long friends,” says Haunani. “They’re going to each other’s second homes and weddings. They travel around the world together.”

The community also organizes annual golf and pickleball tournaments for members, and three golf courses are within a seven-minute drive.

Another incentive is that the community’s zoned as a Vacation Destination Area.

This means properties can join the short-term rental pool, for minimum seven-night stays.

"Some of the owners put their homes in the rental pool and block out time for themselves and their families. That’s a great added benefit," says Haunani, as few club communities on Kauai allow this.

The Sullivans think the buyer will likely hail from the West Coast, enticed by a direct flight from hubs in cities like Los Angeles, San Francisco, Seattle, Phoenix and Denver that arrive on the island by mid-day.

“Kukui‘ula is a great place for families to gather and spend time together. We see a lot of members bring their entire families so they can vacation together multiple times per year,” says Pancho.

“With all these activities it’s easy to curate an incredible vacation with the convenience of being on property. A lot of people enjoy coming here and decompressing.”

Based in Milwaukee, Kristine Hansen writes for TravelandLeisure.com, ArchitecturalDigest.com, Vogue.com, and other outlets. She’s also the author of “Frank Lloyd Wright’s Wisconsin: How America’s Most Famous Architect Found Inspiration in His Home State.” She lives in a 1924 bungalow.

macOS 27.3 could change how you grant AI agents Full Disk Access, here’s how

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Last month, Apple announced it’d be tightening privacy controls in a future macOS update, in response to AI agents requesting Full Disk Access then going rogue with users data without them being fully aware. Now, we have an idea of how the changes might look.

In its blog post, Apple announced that apps will require “very explicit user action” to have Full Disk Access on your Mac. At the time of the announcement, Apple didn’t make it super clear what that would actually mean. Now, we have an idea of what’s to come.

Code sifter pdfu posted on X that Apple will be getting rid of a clever loophole that developers could use to make it easier for users to enable Full Disk Access. In current versions of macOS, if an app requests a file that would require Full Disk Access, the system denies the request and logs the app as an option in the Settings app for enabling Full Disk Access.

At that point, developers often prompt users to open that section of the Settings app and flip the toggle.

Starting as soon as macOS 27.3, apps that request files outside of the permission scope will no longer get logged, and as a result, won’t show up as an option for quickly toggling Full Disk Access, per leaker pdfu.

macOS 27.3 starts implementing changes to Full Disk Access (FDA). Here’s what ‘very explicit user action’ may mean.When an app tries to read a protected file without FDA, macOS doesn’t prompt the user to grant permission. Instead, it automatically denies access and records that… https://t.co/zjUuEh7EIb

At the moment, developers can still request users to drag an app icon to the Settings panel to enable Full Disk Access, even once the feature flag for the aforementioned change is enabled. To me, it still doesn’t quite feel like you’ll need “very explicit user action” to enable Full Disk Access, but it’s possible this will change in the future. This is all still in development.

All in all, I’m not fully convinced that this solves the core problem. Developers are requesting Full Disk Access because they’d like to tap into things like iMessage, and there hasn’t been a better way of doing so. As long as that demand is there, users will likely still have to enable Full Disk Access and risk all sorts of other data, just so they can share their texts with an agent.

Granted, pdfu also mentions the idea of Private Folders, which could potentially allow users to grant scoped access to just the iMessage database folder. It’s unclear if that’ll be possible since there’s little detail on how Private Folders will work. If that’s the case, then hopefully Full Disk Access can stop being the default permission you have to provide for any personal data.

Despite a new version reportedly being around a corner, I still personally believe AirPods Pro 2 are a great deal. They're already equipped with USB-C, and continually gain new software features. With them consistently going on sale, it's a great purchase to make. Features like Adaptive Audio are incredible.

Michael Carrick has painfully awkward exchange with interviewer over Man United future

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Please refresh the page or navigate to another page on the site to be automatically logged inPlease refresh your browser to be logged in

Carrick appeared to be unimpressed by questioning from Sky Sports after United’s remarkable 4-4 draw with Aston Villa as he gave a series of terse responses

Want to bookmark your favourite articles and stories to read or reference later? Start your Independent Membership today.

Michael Carrick endured an awkward end to a post-match interview as he had an excruciating exchange about his future as Manchester United manager.

Carrick had just watched his United side fight back from 4-2 down in injury time to snatch the unlikeliest of 4-4 draws with Aston Villa, having earlier come back from being 2-0 behind inside five minutes.

It was a real mixed display from the Red Devils, who showed plenty of heart and character but had put themselves in bad positions due to consistently disastrous defending.

The result – combined with their incredible comeback victory against Roma in midweek – has probably saved Carrick’s job, although his United side still head into this international break in the Premier League’s bottom three, lying 18th in the table.

His long-term future appears to very much still be up in the air and his post-match interview on Sky Sports with reporter Patrick Davison was an awkward watch.

Carrick firstly bristled slightly when asked if United are looking at a relegation fight, saying: “No, we’re in November. I don’t like this situation we’re in, I don’t like the results we’ve had but we can definitely do something about it.”

From there, things got worse, as Davison asked a lengthy question suggesting there were clearly going to be conversations about his future during the international break over the next couple of weeks, to which Carrick simply responded: "Is there?"

When it was suggested that one would assume so, Carrick tersely said: “Would you?” and when he was pushed for what he thought about his situation, the Man United boss said to Davison: “You’re telling me, what do you think”.

After that, the interviewer specified that he wanted to know what Carrick expected to happen in the next couple of weeks and he then gave a longer answer admitting that it wasn’t a good situation, the team haven’t won enough games but that he expects to move forward and keep working on the things they’ve been working on until this point.

Back in the Sky Sports studio, pundit Roy Keane was asked for his thoughts on the interview as a whole and the former Man United midfielder compared the current boss to a politician.

He said: “It’s obviously difficult for the manager, he’s under pressure no doubt. He talks a lot but doesn’t say much – maybe that’s a tactic. He’s like a politician.

“He’s clearly under pressure and says he knows there are things to work on but he better get moving on that... This is their worst start in however many years.”

Keane was also critical of United’s “dreadful” defending, although he praised the heart they showed to twice come back from two goals behind.

He added: “I think we’ve said previously there have been question marks over this team but they’re showing a bit of fight, a bit of desire and, as bad as the game was from a footballing point of view, they kept going to the end.

“So, on the one hand, you say dreadful to give up four goals at home, but they kept going, so we have to give them a little bit of praise.

“In terms of the bigger picture, it’s that rollercoaster of watching United. Some bits were so, so bad. Defensively the way it started, the start of the second half, but they found a way to get a point in a game where they looked dead and buried.

“Still nowhere near good enough to try and get towards the middle of the table.”

Battlefield 6 Loses Court Case Over Ban

📅 2026-11-08  |  Read Source →

Michael Manning (He/Him) has been enamored with video games since he first played the Sonic Classic Collection as a child. With decades of experience in gaming and a full tour as a Gaming Journalist, Michael is ready to step up to the plate. From AAA to the smallest passion projects, his motto is that every piece of ar

Modern Warfare 4's launch party is still going, with a free play period for DMZ ready to rake in new players.

Back in early August, a Battlefield 6 player named Rychadv complained on Reddit that his profile has been randomly, unexplainably banned after a period of inactivity, with no support levied from EA after attempts at appeal. Today, he revealed that he took EA to court over the case and won, regaining access to his account effective immediately.

While most don’t carefully read the terms and conditions many online games ask of you, the agreement does serve as somewhat legally binding, acting as a way for the studio running the game to establish when and if they can pull your plug. Cheating, improper communications use, or any step out of line with the terms laid out can be used as a justification to temporarily, or permanently, ban you.

The odd part that swung this case fully in Rychadv’s favor was the lack of an actual motive. For no discernible reason, after an extended break from the game, DICE had simply banned his account, with no means of reacquiring it or fighting the ban. Especially on a full-priced title like Battlefield 6, this type of hiccup is strictly unacceptable, potentially costing Rychadv a whole new copy if he didn’t drag DICE to court.

Bans need to be carefully considered by studios before they’re implemented; otherwise, they risk both stepping on toes and unjustly kicking out solid chunks of their populace. The threat of bans, more so than the bans themselves, is often enough to keep the problem children in line. We’ve all had a mouthy, rude player on the team, the more invested in the game they are, the less likely they are to get heinous.

Without them, and without proper explanations for bans, companies can get into more than just PR trouble.

This widely depends on both the player culture and ban frequency of the game itself, and is far more common in free-to-play titles where accounts can quickly rise and fall. For full-priced experiences, developers need to be a bit more careful with the ban hammer, and they especially need to make sure any perceived bannable offenses actually happened.

The gradual move away from physical media for the majority of the gaming industry makes the threat of random bans a bit more serious, especially with cases like Rychadv’s still out in the open. It’s not just multiplayer titles that are always online, and who's to say some studio couldn’t kick you out for what you do in a single-player game in the future, even when it's only affecting your copy?

This is definitely stepping into the speculative, but it's important to remember that digital titles aren’t really yours; you simply buy a license from the studio to play. Without the promise of physical media, there’s a certain formlessness to the games we buy, like they could slip away at any moment.

With no promise of actual ownership, situations like Rychadv’s could happen more and more, putting the onus of action onto the players to hold studios over the fire a bit. When you agree to the terms of service, make sure to read up on it. While you’re playing by the studio’s rules, they need to respect them as well, especially for titles with a price tag.

Improper use of bans could lead to more legal disputes like Rychadv’s, as players become more willing to hold studios to their word.

Deadlock is starting to suffer from the same fate as other free-to-play games, with bot farmers crawling from the woodwork.
